#6796. The Apple Watch Deals You Need to Look for This Black Friday

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

We’re expecting record-low deals in the coming weeks—but for now, we’ll take $250 off.

It’s no secret that Apple has been dominating tech headlines this year. But between the news of its recent price hikes on MacBooks and iPads and the release of the iPhone Duo (the tech giant’s first foldable), not much fanfare has been made about the new generations of the Apple Watch. As far as we’re concerned, that’s a damn shame, because they’re well worth the upgrade—especially if you can find one on sale like we just did.

Apple Watches usually go on deepest discount during Amazon sales, Black Friday included. And while only two models are marked down right now (the Ultra 4 and Ultra 3), we know a thing or two about how Apple deals work. In the coming weeks, prices will drop and these wearables will sell out like hotcakes, which is why you’ll want to plan what you’re going to buy now.

Below, we’re breaking down the differences between each model—and peeling back the curtain on our price-tracking prowess—so you’re all set come Black Friday. If you don’t take the plunge on one before then, that is.

Commerce editor Luke Guillory argued that compared to other fitness trackers on the market, Apple was lagging behind with its lack of readiness score functionality—until now. The Apple Watch Series 12 marks the debut of the brand’s very own readiness score thanks to a revamped tracking system. Plus, the watch now measures your heart rate more frequently to deliver Heart Rate Variability (HRV) readings every five minutes. If you’re trying to pay closer attention to your health this year, it’s a smart buy, made all the sweeter when it inevitably goes back on sale for Black Friday.

For October Prime Day, this brand-new watch surprisingly dropped to just $350, so we’re expecting a similar—if not deeper—discount in the coming weeks.

While the Apple Watch Ultra 4 isn’t necessarily reinventing the wheel, it did get the same advanced health tracking and readiness score features from the Apple Watch Series 12, so if you’re looking to get your first Ultra model, it’s worth grabbing—especially when it’s $100 off. Not to mention, Guillory is partial to the new ocean-inspired, semi-translucent rubber watch bands.

That said, if you’re not someone who needs the shiny new gadget immediately or who won’t take advantage of these souped-up health features—you’re not going to miss out on much if you score an older model on sale, like this $250 discount on the Apple Watch Ultra 3. Associate Commerce Editor Bryn Gelbart went so far as to dub it the “best Apple Watch ever” when it was released last fall.

“I was never tempted to upgrade to the premium option, but the Apple Watch Ultra 3 changed my mind,” Gelbart said. “It turns out I didn't quite know what I was missing.” It has a bright 49mm display, satellite messaging features, a durable titanium case, and a long lasting battery (an impressive 42 hours under normal use and 72 hours on Low Power Mode). Gelbart recommends it for cyclists, runners, or the adventure-prone.

At 28% off, this is the best pre-Black Friday deal to shop as of publication, although this model has dipped as low as $600 before.

If you’re looking for Apple’s most affordable watch, we suggest turning your attention to the entry-level Apple Watch SE 3. It was actually the watch that converted Gelbart to Apple wearables in the first place, arguing that its beauty is in its simplicity.

“It's an accurate and predictive workout tracker. It counts my steps and activity and connects to my phone to play music and receive messages and calls,” he says. “If you’re new to the Apple Watch and want to dip your toes, this is where I recommend you start.” It has the same sleep tracking features, like nightly sleep score and sleep apnea notifications, but lacks the ECG app or hypertension notifications of its newer siblings. It also has an 18-hour battery life so you’ll need to charge most nights.

This dropped to a record-low $199 during October Prime Day, so we’re hopeful that a similar price cut will emerge for Black Friday, but this is by far the most affordable Apple Watch on the market, even at full price.

Our philosophy when it comes to shopping for Apple tech during Black Friday? If you find the watch with the features you need at a price you can stomach, it’s worth grabbing quickly. During Prime Day, a handful of watches sold out completely—and you won’t want your wearable of choice to meet the same fate before you check out.

#6795. The “Facial in a Bottle” Products Experts Say Actually Work

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Your trick to recreating a salon glow at home.

First came “botox in a bottle.” Now, “facial in a bottle” is the latest catchy phrase permeating beauty aisles. Also sometimes labeled with the term “flash facial,” these “multi-functional formulas are designed to mimic the actual steps of a professional facial—think exfoliation, hydration, and brightening—all in one,” explains dermatologist Dr. Jessie Cheung. “The best ones do this by combining acids, enzymes, antioxidants, and hydrators to address multiple skin concerns while minimizing irritation.”

And while they’re not a replacement for in-office treatments and an esthetician’s skilled hand, they can “deliver instant or dramatic results, typically imparting a glow or resurfacing effect,” says New York-based facialist Samantha Susca. She adds that these multipurpose formulas can nicely complement salon visits and help extend results between appointments.

Ahead, we tapped our experts and fellow dermatologist Dr. Pearl Ugwu-Dike for their top “facial in a bottle” recommendations, and included a few picks our beauty editors use religiously. Read on for the products they say truly live up to the hype, including ones Susca uses with her own clients. Plus, expert tips for choosing the right formula for your skin type.

The path to glassier skin often starts with a good cleanser. Susca loves this option for “a gentle daily resurfacing,” noting that it helps clear congestion and remove dead skin while also hydrating, de-puffing, and reducing the look of hyperpigmentation. “Two enzymes gently exfoliate to dissolve both oil and surface keratin, while the sugar-bound vitamin C molecule (ascorbyl glucoside) is one of the most intelligent deliveries of vitamin C that brightens the skin,” she explains. “It develops into a glow-inducing, active vitamin C once the cleanser is rinsed off.” Meanwhile, ectoin, niacinamide, and caffeine help soothe the skin and reduce inflammation.

According to the brand, the water-activated, powder-to-liquid formula is best used once daily. Those with sensitive skin should start by using it for 30 seconds every other day and watch how their skin reacts before potentially increasing the frequency.

Key ingredients: Alpha hydroxy acid complex, vitamin C complex, niacinamide, ectoin, caffeine

Over-the-counter chemical peels can offer some of the powerful skin-evening effects of a professional peel. And because they’re less intense, they can be a great option for those seeking a milder treatment. “The best at-home formulas balance exfoliation with barrier support, keeping your skin brighter, smoother, and more even without irritation,” Dr. Cheung advises. She especially likes this option from Kiehl’s, which she says “combines salicylic acid, glycolic acid, and lactic acid with soothing copper gluconate for a treatment that can be used daily.”

Key Ingredients: salicylic acid, glycolic acid, lactic acid, copper gluconate

A favorite among dermatologists, estheticians, A-listers, and beauty editors, Biologique Recherche’s Lotion P50 is iconic for good reason. “This multi-acid product exfoliates while improving skin function, for immediate glow and long-term improvements,” says Dr. Cheung. The milky toner combines AHAs, BHAs, and vinegar with gluconolactone, a gentler exfoliant, and niacinamide. Together, these ingredients help remove dead skin cells, support cell turnover, regulate sebum production, and balance the skin’s pH. As the most active of the five formulas in the P50 line, it’s best suited for those with thicker or keratinized skin who can handle a stronger exfoliant. Those with thinner or more sensitive skin may prefer P50W, formulated for sensitive, oily skin, or the newer P50T.

In her review of Lotion P50 PIGM 400, formulated to target acne scars and dark marks, contributing fashion and luxury editor Moriah Polk wrote: “About a month into using P50 consistently, I walked into work, and a fellow editor stopped me mid-sentence to say my skin was glowing. And no, I wasn’t wearing makeup. What this product does best is deliver that elusive, youthful luminosity that doesn’t always photograph well but looks incredible IRL. My skin just looks fresher, brighter, and more awake—no small feat in winter, when it’s usually flaky, dull, and generally uncooperative. P50 PIGM 400 is the only thing I’ve changed in my routine, and since starting it, I haven’t had a single breakout.”

Key ingredients: gluconolactone, tartaric acid, citric acid, lactic acid, malic acid, salicylic acid, acetic acid, niacinamide

“This waxless, weightless balm-to-oil has quickly become a desert island product of mine, and a standard finishing touch in my treatments,” says Susca. “The 86 antioxidants leave the most incredible glow that mimics a 90-minute facial.” Using a blend of fruit extracts, seed oils, and emollient-rich murumuru butter, it works to protect and deeply nourish the skin while keeping it calm and balanced.

Bonus: You can also use this to prime the skin for a gua sha session.

Key ingredients: Murumuru butter, elderberry fruit extract, blueberry seed oil, green tea leaf extract

“At just $30, these peeling pads take a remarkably comprehensive approach,” says Dr. Ugwu-Dike. “The first step includes five different exfoliating acids: glycolic, lactic, mandelic, phytic acid, and gluconolactone to help remove dead skin cells and improve texture. The second step introduces retinol to smooth fine lines and ectoin to help soothe and hydrate. Rather than relying on exfoliation alone, it combines immediate surface refinement with an ingredient that has established long-term benefits for photoaging.”

Key ingredients: Glycolic, lactic, mandelic, phytic acid, and gluconolactone, retinol, ectoin

Arguably one of the best parts of a facial is leaving with a bouncier, more lifted complexion. Susca calls Ourself’s serum a standout pick thanks to its “deep hydration delivery system,” which she says “provides a deeper lifting and plumping effect while giving the skin a lit-from-within glow.” This system combines dual-weight hyaluronic acid with a booster intended to encourage the skin’s natural production of the ingredient, and an “HA protector” designed to help it stay in the skin for longer. The formula also contains bioactive peptides, said to stimulate collagen and elastin production, and brightening vitamin C and niacinamide.

Additionally, its patented delivery technology targets specific areas of the skin for the most visible impact. In two-week, company-sponsored clinical trials, “this serum has been proven to volumize areas that are losing depth, smooth fine lines, and improve tone with consistent use,” Susca notes.

Key ingredients: Bioactive peptides, HA+ complex, vitamin C, niacinamide, patented tiered-release vesicle technology

For a quick, facial-like reset, ELLE beauty commerce editor Nerisha Penrose swears by this multitasking Korean toner mist, formulated to nourish and calm skin, minimize the appearance of pores, and even out tone.

“I stay away from most buzzy skin care because I’m on a derm-prescribed plan to help erase acne breakouts and post-acne scarring. On the nights when I’m not using my retinol or topical antibiotics, I’m generously spritzing my skin with this deeply hydrating mist,” says Penrose. “The gentle formula combines soothing botanicals and antioxidants that deliver moisture, relief, and barrier strength over time. By the time I wake up, I have the most supple, softest, radiant skin.”

Key ingredients: Rose water, lactobacillus ferment, betaine, melin seed extract, ivy seed extract

“This serum is a fantastic example of exfoliation and hydration working beautifully together, and is one of the best options for dry, mature skin,” says Dr. Ugwu-Dike. “It contains 10 percent lactic acid, which helps loosen the bond between dead skin cells to improve surface texture. But what really makes this formula impactful is the inclusion of ceramides, hyaluronic acid, and glycerin, which help support moisture retention.”

Key ingredients: Lactic acid, ceramides, hyaluronic acid, glycerin

“You want a balanced mix of actives and barrier support,” says Dr. Cheung. “For exfoliation, look for AHAs (lactic, glycolic), BHAs (salicylic acid), or enzymes. For brightening, look for vitamin C, niacinamide, or tranexamic acid. For hydration and barrier support, you want hyaluronic acid, glycerin, and ceramides.”

“If you’re oily or prone to acne, go for BHAs or a combination of exfoliants,” she continues. “If you’re a dry or sensitive type, go for lactic acid or enzymes with more hydration.”

Dr. Ugwu-Dike adds: “Alpha hydroxy acids are some of my favorite ingredients when it comes to achieving that immediate glow. Glycolic acid has a small molecular size, which makes it really effective for treating photo damage and uneven texture. Lactic acid offers both exfoliating and humectant properties, which makes it excellent for somebody whose skin is also dehydrated.”

All our experts agree that it’s best to look for options with multiple active ingredients at lower, “more tolerable” percentages, especially if you have reactive skin. “High-acid concentrations can cause inflammation, which can trigger post-inflammatory hyperpigmentation that can lead to dark marks and skin scarring,” warns Dr. Ugwu-Dike.

Resist the urge to overdo it, Dr. Cheung advises. “Focus on using these with purpose, not just piling on more products. Apply after cleansing, starting once or twice a week, then increasing as tolerated. Don’t stack multiple strong actives the same night to avoid irritation.”

“Hydration is the important finishing touch,” adds Dr. Ugwu-Dike. “I love pairing an exfoliating treatment with a rich, non-irritating moisturizer because it helps create that smooth, glass-like finish people associate with facials.”

As with any skin care product, she notes, the best results come with consistency.

Dr. Jessie Cheung is a board-certified dermatologist with practices in Chicago, Illinois, and New York City.

Samantha Susca is an esthetician and founder of Refinery4 in New York City.

Dr. Pearl Ugwu-Dike is a NYC-based board-certified dermatologist.

She also included holy grail picks from ELLE Beauty Commerce Editor Nerisha Penrose and Fashion & Luxury Contributing Editor Moriah Polk.

#6794. 33 Bedroom Plants That Bring a Calming Touch to Your Space

Do you have one of these in your room yet?

Want to improve your sleep naturally? Try adding a few plants to your bedroom. Beyond offering charm and a cozy atmosphere, plants have been linked to mental and physical benefits, including sleep. Research suggests that being around greenery can lift your mood, ease stress, and promote a greater sense of well-being. Bringing a touch of nature into your sleeping space may be a simple way to add those positive qualities to the room.

Plants may also help calm the mind and move the body from an alert state toward a more restful one, potentially supporting better sleep. A past study linked indoor greenery with more positive emotions and fewer negative ones, which may help you fall asleep faster. The findings point to a broader connection between greenery and well-being, including its possible role in a restful bedroom. It’s easy to see why biophilic design—an approach that evokes nature indoors—has become a favorite among designers.

Ahead, find the best plants for your bedroom. We made sure the options would thrive in all types of lights, but especially in low light, so that whether you’re a beginner or an experienced green thumb, you will find the right verdant friend to grow.

The Aspidistra elatior thrives in low to bright shade, making it perfect for bedrooms that don’t get too much direct light. A fun fact: This plant historically was referred to as the “bar room” plant or “beer plant” because it could survive smoky, low-light conditions in Victorian-era taverns and halls. It is non-toxic to humans, cats, and dogs so if you have pets this is a great option.

Native to the rainforests of Brazil, the Maranta leuconeura is now a common indoor houseplant. During the day, the oval leaves lie flat to soak up the light and at night, the leaves fold upward like praying hands, hence the name.

For a trailing plant that thrives in bright, indirect light get a wax plant. It appreciates higher humidity levels so if you live in an older home with fluctuating humidity, this is the verdant plant baby for you.

For beginner and intermediate plant owners, the nerve plant is a great option because they thrive in most indoor conditions, remain compact, and are safe for pets. They will signal to you what they need so you can water and choose the soil for it accordingly.

Pilea peperomioides, or the Chinese money plant, is a cheerful-looking, easy-to-propagate plant that is incredibly low-maintenance. It's a great plant for a sunny bedroom. All plant parents need to do is place it in bright, indirect sunlight and water only when the top inch of soil feels dry.

Aglaonema tolerates very low light levels, so it's ideal if you don't have large windows in your bedroom. Water it when the top few inches of soil feel dry.

The snake plant doesn't mind low light—and because it's a succulent, it only needs to be watered every week or two.

Purported to bring good luck, the money tree has a nice, modern vibe—plus, it's not fussy and grows quickly. Give it medium light, water it every week or two, and it'll be happy.

The pothos plant tolerates any light level from low to high. (That means it's super-easy to keep alive!) Just don't water it until the top few inches of soil feel dry to the touch—overwatering is the only real way to kill this plant.

Lavender has long been considered beneficial for relaxation and sleep. Although most research has studied the use of lavender essential oils specifically, having a live lavender plant in your bedroom probably can't hurt. It needs bright light, so make sure it's on a sunny windowsill or use a grow light. Water when the top inch or two feels dry to the touch.

This handsome vining plant is a great choice for new plant parents. Its glossy, heart-shaped leaves are beautiful as they spill over the edges of nightstands or bookshelves–and it's tolerant of low light levels. Water when the top few inches of soil feel dry.

This adorable fern is one of the best to raise indoors. Its thick, waxy leaves retain water better than many other ferns that have finer fronds—and it won't leave a mess by dropping foliage every day. Give it bright indirect light and water when the top inch of soil feels dry.

This fun plant looks like an old-fashioned ric rac trim. It will grow like crazy in bright indirect light—and because it's a succulent, it needs watering only every week to 10 days.

This social media darling is striking and easy to grow—it just needs bright indirect light. Water when the top few inches of soil feel dry.

The peace lily will adapt well to low light levels, but it needs bright indirect light to bloom. Water when the top inch or two feel dry.

This cute palm that looks just like a messy ponytail likes bright indirect light. And the bulbous base stores water, so it needs a drink only every few weeks when the soil is totally dry.

This handsome plant boasts a beautiful upright form, so it makes an eye-catching statement in the bedroom. Give it bright indirect light, and water when the top few inches of soil are dry. As an outdoor landscape plant, it will flower, though that's unusual indoors.

If you're looking for low-maintenance houseplants, this is the way to go! It will tolerate low light and is often grown in water. In water, change it weekly; in soil, water so that it stays slightly moist.

This cute tabletop palm will add loads of style to your bedroom. It prefers bright indirect light, but it will tolerate medium light better than many other types of palms. Water when it's slightly dry to the touch.

#6793. All of Princess Kate’s Remembrance Sunday Looks Over the Years

The Princess of Wales first attended the event in 2011

Princess Catherine has joined the royal family for the National Service of Remembrance every year since 2011, the same year that she got married to Prince William. The somber annual ceremony, held on Remembrance Sunday at the Cenotaph, a war memorial in London, honors service members who have lost their lives at war. It takes place on the second Sunday of November, and it involves a church service, a wreath-laying ceremony, and a formal procession, all of which are attended by various members of the British royal family.

When it comes to her royal style, the Princess of Wales has long embraced a more sustainable approach, frequently rewearing dresses, jewelry, and accessories. Over the years, that style philosophy has been on full display at Remembrance Sunday, where Kate has made a tradition of recycling looks and adding sentimental touches through meaningful brooches and heirloom jewelry.

One of her signature accessories is the red poppy pin, a symbol that has come to be widely associated with the holiday. Kate is often seen wearing three poppies at once, and while the royal family has never confirmed the significance behind the number, one theory suggests it may be a personal nod to her great-grandmother’s three brothers, all of whom lost their lives during World War I.

Throughout the years, Kate has also worn pieces that once belonged to late royal family members like Queen Elizabeth II and Princess Diana, which she has styled with outfits from Catherine Walker, Alexander McQueen, Temperley London, Diane von Furstenberg, and more.

Ahead, take a look back at Princess Kate’s Remembrance Sunday style through the years, from her first appearance in 2011 to now. And be sure to check back for more updates once the Princess of Wales steps out for the 2026 ceremony, too.

For her first appearance with the royal family at the Cenotaph memorial, Kate wore a black Diane von Furstenberg coat adorned with a double poppy pin along with a Jane Corbett hat and Kiki McDonough earrings.

At the 2012 Remembrance Sunday ceremony, the then-Duchess of Cambridge rewore her Diane von Furstenberg coat from the previous year, this time paired with a Philip Treacy hat, Annoushka earrings, and a single sparkling poppy brooch.

Opting for a different outerwear look for her third appearance, Kate wore a single-breasted Temperley London coat, which she accessorized with a Sylvia Fletcher for Lock & Co. hat and Kiki McDonough hoop earrings with Annoushka pearl drops.

In 2014, Kate wore a black Alexander McQueen jacket over a black ruffled BCBG dress. She rewore the Jane Corbett hat she donned in 2011, and she added a floral brooch, a Mappin & Webb diamond necklace, and Princess Diana’s sapphire earrings.

Kate stepped out in another Alexander McQueen coat for the 2015 Remembrance Day service, which featured velvet trim along the collar, sleeves, and waist. The then-duchess topped it off with a Lock & Co. hat adorned with a sculptural butterfly and black veil, the Kiki McDonough hoop earrings and Annoushka pearl drops she’d worn in 2013, and a triple-poppy pin on her lapel.

In another rewear, Kate donned the same Diane von Furstenberg coat she wore to her first and second Remembrance Sunday services in 2011 and 2012. This time, she styled it with a John Boyd hat, diamond-and-pearl earrings, and three poppies on her lapel.

Kate switched up her look for the 2017 National Service of Remembrance, opting for a double-breasted, collarless Dolce & Gabbana coat with oversized gold buttons, statement Oscar de la Renta earrings in gold, and a Philip Treacy hat she wore in 2006, during her early days of dating Prince William. She also debuted a new hair look, pinning it up to create the illusion of a short bob.

The 2018 Remembrance Day Service marked a significant milestone: the 100th anniversary of the end of World War I. For the occasion, Kate wore a custom Alexander McQueen jacket with military-inspired details, including a high white collar, red shoulder embellishments, and crest-style buttons. She completed the look with a Lock & Co. hat, a triple red poppy pin, and a brooch honoring the women who served during World War I.

Opting for another military-inspired jacket, Kate wore a Catherine Walker coat and a Philip Treacy hat featuring a black veil. Forgoing her usual poppy pin, she instead wore a special pin known as the “Codebreaker’s Brooch” in honor of her grandmother who worked at Bletchley Park during World War II.

At the socially distanced 2020 Remembrance Day ceremony, held amid the COVID-19 pandemic, Kate once again opted for a military-inspired look. She wore a Catherine Walker jacket with fringe detailing at the shoulders, complete with a Philip Treacy fascinator, pearl drop earrings, and her signature triple-poppy pin.

Kate brought back her Alexander McQueen coat from the 2018 service for the 2021 Remembrance Day ceremony. She paired the repeat look with Princess Diana’s pearl earrings, a wide-brimmed Lock & Co. hat, and her signature triple-poppy pin, along with a crystal flower brooch from the Royal British Legion.

For her first Remembrance Day Service following Queen Elizabeth II’s passing, the Princess of Wales wore a black Catherine Walker coat, Princess Diana’s pearl-and-diamond earrings, and a wide-brimmed Philip Treacy hat. She completed the look with her signature trio of poppies, paired with an Art Deco-inspired diamond brooch.

The Princess of Wales opted for multiple rewears at the 2023 Remembrance Sunday service. She repeated the custom Catherine Walker coat she first wore in 2019 and the Philip Treacy hat she previously wore at the 2012 and 2017 services. Then, for some added sparkle, she wore Queen Elizabeth’s diamond-and-pearl earrings, and her signature three-poppy pin.

Kate didn’t make many public appearances in 2024 due to her cancer diagnosis, but she still attended the Remembrance Sunday service that year. She wore a Catherine Walker coat dress, featuring a velvet bow collar, and she accessorized with a Juliette Botterill hat, Queen Elizabeth’s earrings, and her triple-poppy pin.

Kate’s look at the 2025 Remembrance Sunday service featured a rewear with a new twist. She opted for the same Catherine Walker coat from 2022, but this time, she styled it with a lace navy-blue pie-crust collar blouse draping out of the top. She accessorized with a sculptural Lock & Co. fascinator, Princess Diana’s pearl-drop earrings, a single poppy brooch, and a Royal Air Force brooch.

#6792. These 9 Brands Are Making the Best Dark-Wash Jeans on the Market

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Moody, rich, and sophisticated—everything you want to project with your dressing.

In the world of denim, think of dark wash jeans like a good Scotch. It exists within a broader umbrella, but it’s a little more weathered and more mature. In the grand scheme of jeans, you really can’t go wrong when it comes to different washes. For clarity, we use dark wash to describe lightly washed dark jeans, raw sanforized jeans, and the real nerdy loomstate shrink-to-fit stuff. Why? Because they are all, visually, dark wash. For us, it’s exactly how you should buy your jeans.

Jeans inherently skew casual. But the subdued color of dark wash allows it to pass for something a little more refined. Where other denim gets processed and faded after being dyed, dark wash hasn’t—letting it keep that subdued inky hue that veers slightly toward “polished trouser” territory. They’re more rugged than a pair of slacks, but just formal and sophisticated enough that you can probably wear them around the office as a sort of nonchalantly edgy work pant.

You won’t need to hunt far or hard for quality and stylish dark washes. Most, if not all, of the Esquire-editor-approved jeans and denim brands also offer darker colors—making it as easy as buying your favorite sweater in another color. Find your favorite below.

Rag & Bone knows what it’s good at and sticks to it. (It’s denim...they’re good at denim.) Our reviewers especially like how the brand approaches its sizing and fits. It’s a super simple and straightforward number system that starts at 1 and goes up to 4. Fit 1 offers a slimmer silhouette, while Fit 4 gives that classic straight look.

For their dark wash—which Rag & Bone calls “Solomon”—the brand uses its very own rb Selvedge, which it developed in partnership with one of Japan's premier denim mills. This will help the jeans naturally develop a nice patina over time to really show just how much you love your denim.

If you ask Esquire shopping editor Luke Guillory what jeans to buy, he’s going to tell you these. He’s already waxed poetic about Wrangler’s original Cowboy Cut in our denim buying guide, so we’ll just cut to the chase: they’re super flattering and at an ultra-competitive price (though he advises bigger guys should opt for the more relaxed cut).

If you’re looking for something under $100, Gap’s more than likely where it’s at. The retailer has spent a ton of time and effort in reupping its basics—jeans especially. The brand’s classic straight silhouette is, at this point in time, better than the offering from the big-name brand that starts with “L” and rhymes with WiFi.

People hunt for vintage Levi’s for a reason. These 505s are a reproduction of the brand’s iconic style for 1967. As Luke describes in our denim buying guide, the fit will “hug up top, drape down low.” “They're universally flattering and classic cut, so you don't have to worry about them going out of style,” he says. “As long as they're holding on, you'll look good.”

We’ve already rounded up a few Todd Snyder pieces we’d recommend buying, and these dark indigo worn wash jeans are one more thing to add to the list. Available in three different fits—slim, straight, and relaxed—it’s made from an all-cotton denim that balances comfort and durability.

This denim from Ralph Lauren’s Americana sublabel is an absolute classic. It’s got a distinctly Western feel with its deep 13.75 oz selvedge denim, while still offering that sort of rugged sophistication you want from a dark wash jean.

Little known fact, but Acne Studios actually started out with jeans. Over 20 years later, it’s still their bread and butter—with funky pieces that embody a “rock and roll edge,” as Luke describes them. This groovy wide-legged pair especially gives off a sort of “Lenny-Kravitz-meets-Steven-Tyler” air of vintage swagger.

We’re long-time fans of Buck Mason. As Esquire contributor Matthew Medendrop describes, the brand has been “quietly doing some of the most exciting things in contemporary menswear” for the last few years—and their denim is no exception. The brand’s “full saddle” jeans take inspiration from the classic vintage blue jeans, think Levi’s 501s circa like the ‘60s. Pair that with the deep dark wash, and you got one sexy pair of jeans.

3Sixteen is one of the premier brands for Esquire’s denim nerds. It gets into the nitty-gritty stuff we care about, but since it’s based here in North America—and not in Japan—it’s a lot easier to get your hands on. To break down the naming, RS means relaxed straight, what we think is the easiest to wear from the brand. The 100xk tag is the type of denim—custom woven for the brand by Kabuki Mills. It’s rough at first, be warned, but it softens and fades like no other.

#6788. Our All-Time Favorite Saatva Mattress Is 20% Off

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

You’d have to be living under a rock to miss all of this year’s early Black Friday deals. But the real news is that Saatva, our favorite mattress company, is in the mix. In the spirit of mattress selling, no deal can go unchallenged, so Saatva is trying to get one over on Amazon, Wayfair, and other major retailers by offering Esquire readers an exclusive 20 percent off mattress purchases over $1,000, through December 1.

Beating the holiday rush can save you up to $640 on the Saatva Classic, “the best all-around mattress you can buy on the market today,” according to commerce editor Luke Guillory. “This is not a memory-foam mattress that’s meant to be all squishy and fall apart in a year,” Guillory writes in his Classic mattress review. “This is a real-deal mattress with metal. It will, I’m certain, last longer than similarly priced models.”

The mattress comes in three firmness levels: plush soft, luxury firm, and firm. Guillory chose the advertised middle-ground luxury firm, since his wife prefers a sink-in bed while he’d sleep on a Japanese-style floor mattress if he had his way. “The soft, quilted cotton pillow top is breathable but cradles you to sleep,” he said. “Beneath that, a lumbar support zone made out of gel gives you structure in the part of the bed that gets the most wear and tear, and it gives the mattress a bit of pushback. (This was a must-have for me.)” It was great for him, but he noted it was a bit too firm for his wife. If you’re undecided, you can take Saatva’s handy mattress quiz, or go get a nostalgic mattress-hopping experience if you live close to a brick-and-mortar showroom.

The Saatva Classic may be the brand’s most popular mattress, but it’s not the only one with rave reviews. Models tailored toward back pain, larger bodies, and hot sleepers are also discounted by 20 percent, equaling savings of up to $1,000—money that could go a long way when gift shopping.

Every Saatva mattress purchase includes free delivery and setup, and they’ll even haul away your old mattress free of charge. You can rest easy (pardon the pun) knowing you have a full 365 days to switch to a different firmness level or mattress size—or receive a full refund.

This exclusive deal runs through December 1, so you still have plenty of time to decide. With that being said, you could avoid a whole lot of sleepless nights between now and then. Plus, if you spring for a new guest bedroom mattress ahead of Thanksgiving, you’ll impress the hell out of your overnight guests. The only downside? They may never want to leave.

#6787. Medallion Belts Are Everywhere

Consider it a chicer take on the old Western belt.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

If the runways are any indication, we’ve all been loving a good, impactful belt. Marc Jacobs loaded models up with 2008-coded chain belts at his most recent show, while McQueen wrapped black leather creations around the waist, which also snaked up to the neck like collar harnesses. Celine presented hulking supersize belts that cemented creative director Michael Rider’s dedication to standout accessorizing for spring/summer 2027. Yet at the center of it all lives the medallion belt. Consider it the most wearable version of the statement belt trend.

The look is an easy way to accessorize instantly. Medallion belts act as a kind of jewelry in this case, with their chunky hardware leading the way. Whether paired with jeans and a T-shirt or slung over a little black dress, they’re both practical and slightly whimsical. Both Dior and Celine have put an emphasis on the medallion silhouette, with chic, polished medal belt buckles engraved with designer logos.

Gilded in gold tones and attached to luscious leather, the designer medallions act as modern cartouches, proudly displaying the brand’s name like a badge. It’s definitely a modern take on logomania. Dior has also used the same medallion design seen on its belts in its beauty products, proving there’s always a desire for cute, shiny things. If the shape feels inherently Marie Antoinette-ified, it’s intentional. Its inspiration draws from the 18th-century Rococo aesthetic of Dior’s couture salons.

Chloé too has released a medallion belt. While it’s less logo-driven, it has all the major hallmarks that make the look both elevated and impactful. Four golden, gleaming horse heads surround a talismanic design. The horse motif is a clear nod to the bohemian style that is closely tied to the fashion house’s history. It also heavily relates to the lineage of the medallion belt itself, which has been around for centuries (from ancient Rome’s military uniforms and beyond) but thrived during the 1970s when Western wear reached a peak obsession. The rounded shapes of modern medallion belts of today also reference the original Native American concho belts, which originated in the American Southwest during the late 1860s and blended Spanish-Mexican aesthetics.

What sets the medallion belt apart from some of the other trending belts of the moment is its inherent ease and practicality. Some brands are even taking things a step further. For Givenchy’s spring/summer 2027 show, creative director Sarah Burton gave the medallion belt a makeover by turning the gilded emblem into a usable compact mirror. These days, the medallion belt is a real rarity in fashion: it’s an opulent staple that’s actually practical, too.

Valentino Garavani Butterfly-Buckle Leather Belt

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Fun-size favorites from Prada, Rare Beauty, and more make perfect holiday stocking stuffers

The ideas that things can be “small but mighty” and that “good things come in small packages” are especially true for your favorite beauty products. There’s plenty of room, metaphorically and literally, to curate a collection of the best beauty minis, perfect for packing in your carry-on or gifting to friends and family for the holidays.

Itty-bitty, often limited-edition versions of rotation staples you know and love are simply adorable, like Rare Beauty by Selena Gomez Soft Pinch Liquid Blush and Prada’s tiniest set of stylish metal claw clips. Plus, these staples are not only aesthetically pleasing, but also won’t take up too much real estate on your counter or in a festive stocking. If you’re looking to expand your collection of hair, makeup, skincare, and fragrance goodies while keeping it small, I invite you to peruse our short list of the best, too-cute-for-words beauty minis.

Sure, your best friend could use regular bar soap, but why not gift them with this distinguished set of four minis instead? This second edition of Soft Services’s Buffing Bar Miniature Collection might be even better than the first and is entirely dedicated to gourmand scents. From the vanilla-coconut cream, espresso-, and citrus-scented Affogato L'Orange to their best-selling and extremely fresh Green Banana bar to try, and more, chances are your friend will be taking a bunch of calls from the bathtub.

#6784. The Fringe Pieces Fashion Insiders Are Wearing This Fall

Runways say this is the adornment of the season.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Fringe never really goes out of style. No matter the season, you can always expect to see the embellishment swaying from the arm of a suede jacket or dangling off a leather bag. So when it comes to trends around the design detail, it’s a mere matter of what form the fringe takes. Lately, fashion’s found itself in a fringe fixation, prompting the trim to move beyond the Western-inspired looks it’s best known for.

Fringe re-emerged on the scene last fall when spring/summer 2026 catwalks from Bottega Veneta, Givenchy, and Chanel showcased the adornment as playful ornamentation for skirts, bags, and even shoes. By the fall/winter 2026 season, others were beginning to chime in with their own takes. There were the boho-inspired bags from Etro, the romantic wispy dresses from Ulla Johnson, and restrained silhouettes from Altuzarra.

Whether you’re looking for a bold statement or a little wink of style, fringe remains a simple way to add flair to any outfit. And right now, there’s a fringed version of just about any wardrobe piece you could think of. Between the classic leather staples and newer avant-garde styles, you’ll find something for every style sensibility.

Do an online search for fringe jackets, and you’ll find no shortage of options to shop. Leather and suede remain tried-and-true materials for this style of outerwear—but now there’s also a plethora of other textiles to shop as well. Opt for Toteme’s viral scarf coat if you’re in the market for a new winter coat, or this tweed number from Etro if you’re looking for a polished piece you can wear to the office.

Nili Lotan Eloi Fringe-Trimmed Suede Jacket

Simon Miller Mabel Vegan Leather Fringe Jacket

If recent fall/winter 2026 runways from Givenchy and Proenza Schouler show us anything, it’s that fringe dresses are reaching new heights. The trim is an especially pretty touch to formalwear and event dresses, providing playful movement with every step to even the simplest silhouettes.

Cara Cara Troy Floral-Print Fringe-Trimmed Cotton-Blend Jersey Midi Dress

Ciebon Nella Asymmetric Fringe Midi Dress

Cult Gaia Noosha Fringed Silk Halterneck Maxi Dress

Just like dresses, fringe skirts are experiencing a similarly sophisticated evolution. Lately, the style has favored sleek satin fringe to create a distinct piece that will immediately elevate anything it’s paired with.

Bottega Veneta Fringed Knitted Silk Midi Skirt

Alaïa Tassle Fringed Asymmetric Wool-Gabardine Midi Skirt

Anna Sui Asymmetric Floral-Flock Fringe-Trimmed Crepe Midi Skirt

The sky’s the limit when it comes to fringe bags these days. There’s long fringe and short fringe. There’s standard leather and suede trim that adds a timeless flair, or more dramatic confections made of sequins, beads, or feathers. With beaded tassel bags on the rise, it makes sense that fringe would take similar cues for an eclectic flourish.

Fendi Croissant Fringed Calf Hair Shoulder Bag

Nili Lotan Asher Fringed-Trimmed Suede Shoulder Bag

After Givenchy debuted a pair of bold fringed mules as part of its spring/summer 2026 collection, fringe exploded onto shoes—heels in particular—in a big way. These Boudoir mules, as they came to be known, introduced a playful take on fringe footwear. Now it’s an ultra-luxe, fashion-forward style that’s perfect for those who never shy away from making a statement.

#6783. Netflix Finally Revealed the First Stranger Things Prequel Spin-off

Just when you think you’re out of the Upside Down, Netflix pulls you back in. On this “Stranger Things Day”—November 6, which marks the date when Will Byers first went missing back in season 1—Netflix announced the first live-action spin-off of its juggernaut hit Stranger Things.

On November 6, Netflix formally announced Other Worlds: A Stranger Things Adventure, with a teaser that confirmed the show will be a prequel set in 1950s Nevada. More specifically, April 19, 1955, in Marion, Nevada. (This also dates the show just before the stage play Stranger Things: The First Shadow, per Netflix’s press release.) The series is currently slated to premiere in 2028.

Netflix revealed little else about the series, except for its creative personnel. The Duffer Brothers are back as executive producers, along with Kate Trefy, who has worked with the Duffers on the original Stranger Things and co-wrote the stage play Stranger Things: The First Shadow. Director Joe Wright, whose feature credits include Pride & Prejudice (2005), Darkest Hour (2017), and Cyrano (2021), will direct the first three episodes. Production is scheduled to take place in New Mexico.

You can watch the teaser below, which shows a child’s bicycle, a portable radio on the fritz, and ominous dark skies over a restricted area. In other words: It’s Stranger Things, just a little bit older.

So, you thought Netflix was done with Stranger Things? You thought wrong! The hit show’s finale, which premiered on New Year’s Eve earlier this year, straight-up crashed the streamer due to a huge spike in user traffic. That’s despite fans and critics feeling split over the show’s resolution and fatigued by its years-long, drawn-out resolution.

This also isn’t new news. The Duffers have publicly discussed spin-offs before, including in an interview with Polygon in December 2025. The news today isn’t “It’s happening,” but confirmation of its title and period setting.

Will Other Worlds captivate audiences like its predecessor? Between this and It: Welcome to Derry, we’ve maybe had enough of spooky things scaring the bejeezus out of suburban kids on bikes. But if it ain’t broke, there’s clearly nothing to fix. We’ll see for sure when the show premieres in 2028.

#6782. These Perfumes Are So Irresistible, Strangers Will Stop You to Ask What You’re Wearing

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Finding your signature scent involves a lot of trial and error—and a tingling nose. Fragrance is powerful, and discovering a unique scent for daily or occasional wear can uplift your mood, evoke a memory, or make your presence known in any room before you even speak. Whether you’re hunting for a fragrance that feels distinctly you or building a perfume wardrobe to ensure you’re prepared for every event on your social calendar, shopping for perfume can feel overwhelming. One moment, everyone’s going for guava; the next, it’s all about sea salt.

Truth is, trends come and go. However, the journey to building a unique collection is about finding a fragrance that truly resonates—whether floral, gourmand, musky, or a distinct layered blend.

“Fragrance has the power to evoke memories of a person, a moment, an experience,” says DSM-Firmenich perfumer Bérénice Watteau. “We’ve all experienced it: walking down the street, catching a familiar scent on a stranger, and suddenly being brought back to someone we love or a beautiful memory shared with them. Perfume captures those fleeting feelings and makes them timeless.” Your scent should also feel like an extension of yourself, something that reflects your personality, style, and the way you want to be remembered. Don’t be afraid to layer a couple of scents for an irreplicable fragrance of your own creation.

As beauty editors, we’re lucky enough to spritz, sniff, and test hundreds of scents, so it takes a lot for one to impress us—that’s how you know the ones on the list below are extra special. We also consulted three experts and combed through numerous customer reviews to find 16 of the best perfumes for women that we can’t stop wearing.

Have you ever wondered what Kim Kardashian’s hair smells like? Well, here’s your answer. She uses the hair mist version of the Valaya Exclusif Eau de Parfum. The formula delivers a fresh, clean finish, anchored by a beautifully balanced blend of bergamot, mandarin, and white peach. Plus, the tassel-adorned bottle looks beautiful on display.

Smells like: A Kardashian’s hair (literally)

Key notes: Bergamot, mandarin, white peach

Customer review: “I’ve never been stopped so much by people asking for what fragrance I’m wearing! This is heavenly, and when you add with the Delina body oil it is really delightful and long lasting. Worth the try!”

I’m a sucker for vanilla fragrances, and this one strikes the perfect balance: sweet but never overbearing, and indulgent without veering into anything overly sugary or childish. Since I started wearing it, the compliments haven’t stopped. While I felt undeniably cool wearing a scent no one else could get their hands on, I’m just as excited for everyone else to finally experience it.”—EAB

Key notes: Almond, coffee, bergamot, lemon, jasmine, orange blossom, tonka bean, cacao, vanilla, sandalwood, patchouli, and cedar

Expert review: “ELLE editors made an entire TikTok about their favorite Good Girl fragrance. This one is perfect for your first date with a guy you met on Hinge. The aroma is warm, sweet, and sexy. I notice that on my friends who tend to run warmer, the sandalwood, vanilla, and orange blossom do a beautiful dance together.”—Danielle James, beauty expert

Baccarat Rouge 540 is arguably one of the most iconic and coveted perfumes of the 21st century. This perfume smells like pure luxury; the slightly sweet, floral scent settles into a spicy, earthy aroma as jasmine, cedar, and saffron take center stage.

Key notes: Jasmine, saffron, cedarwood, ambergris accord

ELLE editor review: “This perfume is a guaranteed conversation starter. The distinct smell either evokes the response of ‘Ahh, I love 540,’ amongst those who know it, or ‘What is that smell?!’ to those who are about to be introduced to the reigning queen of the perfume occult. The smell is intense and sultry without being overbearing.”—Emma Aerin Becker, associate beauty e-commerce writer

Perfumer Ben Gorham captured the essence of the color white by combining delicate florals, a woody base note, and a hint of musk; the scent makes you feel fresh, polished, and put-together, even in 90-degree weather.

I bury my nose in my cotton sheets when I take them out of the dryer, and I bury my nose in my wrist when I wear this perfume.”—Carol Lee, beauty writer

There’s a reason you see this iconic bottle in practically every magazine you’ve ever opened. It’s a classic, and the fruity, floral ingredients never weigh down the luscious elegance of the ylang-ylang, jasmine, pear, and lily.

Key notes: Ylang-ylang, Damascus rose, jasmine

Expert review: “This warm floral somehow smells good on everyone who tries it, and it would be a fantastic gift for any occasion. I gave my roommates (who have very different tastes in fragrance) a bottle each for Christmas, and both of them were beyond delighted.”—CL

Many of Kilian’s scents evoke a boozy night at a sexy bar and make you feel and smell like the main character of any venue you walk into. On the Rocks expands on the sexy swing of the Angels’ Share collection—where Paradis opened with a berry burst, On the Rocks tickles your nose with the rinds of your favorite citruses: mandarin, lemon, grapefruit, and bitter orange. It’s not all fruits, though; the scent settles down into a swig of cognac, tonka bean, and sandalwood without ever overpowering its citrus notes.

Key notes: Italian lemon, grapefruit, bergamot, mandarin, bitter orange, tonka bean, cognac

ELLE editor review: “I feel like I should be in Italy every time I wear On the Rocks. The tangy notes, intermixed with the deeper, sexier ones we know Kilian for, elevate any outfit. I always get compliments around the office whenever I wear this.”—Nerisha Penrose

Think of all the best parts of summer—frozen citrus sorbets, sunny beach days, salty sea air—this perfume combines them into a sunny scent. Never-ending Summer is a perfume that keeps the summer vibes going, with notes of bitter orange, ginger, earl gray, and cedarwood.r

Key notes: Bitter orange, pepper duet, fresh ginger essence, earl grey accord, cardamom, vetiver, cedarwood, patchouli essence

ELLE editor review: “Just as its name suggests, this perfume truly is summer in a bottle. A few spritzes release notes of freshly peeled oranges and bright vetiver, instantly transporting me to a sun-drenched afternoon with an Aperol spritz in hand. As someone who’s perpetually chasing warm weather and carefree summer energy (I am a Leo, after all), this scent will be my go-to long past the season’s end—because sometimes you need a subtle reminder of your favorite time of year.” —EAB

Take a chance (pun intended) on this fruity and floral fragrance. Bursting with notes of jasmine, rose, and grapefruit, it’s slightly sweet yet refreshingly vibrant—prepare to field all of the compliments you’re bound to receive.

ELLE editor review: “Of all the perfumes I’ve tried over the years, Chanel's Chance has remained a constant in my lineup. It’s lighter and more modern than the brand’s iconic N°5 scent, yet is still just as effortlessly classic and long-lasting. In fact, I’ve found it to be such a universal crowd-pleaser that even an Uber driver once asked me what I was wearing.”—Sam Peters, contributor

The whole point of this perfume is that it smells like the best version of you. That is, a you that showers with expensive body wash and never sweats a drop. “I adore skin scents, and this one is musky, elegant, and intimate,” says Watteau. It creates a soft second-skin effect that feels personal and addictive.”

Ouds can be quite intimidating to delve into, especially if your palate has always leaned toward sweet, soft notes. Milky Oud from Kayali meets you in the middle, adding depth to a smoothie of strawberry cream, saffron, coffee, oud, and sandalwood.

Key notes: Oud, strawberry cream, incense, saffron

ELLE editor review: “Kayali’s Oudgasm collection marries the deep, darker aroma of the main ingredient with lighter notes to create everyday scents that turn heads in the best way. My favorite has always been Kayali’s Smoky Oud mixed with Vanilla Oud—Milky Oud is basically its golden child. Even though oud can be heavy in perfume, it softens in this juice with a second skin-like embrace.”—NP

Fame by Rabanne smells like a sexy night out in a beautiful tropical locale—fruity, spicy, and woody, with appearances from mango, incense, and sandalwood. “Gourmands aren’t usually my thing, but this is the only vanilla perfume I reach for over and over, especially for a night on the town,” says beauty writer Carol Lee. “Nerisha keeps a bottle by her desk in the office, and whenever she tries a scent we both can’t stand, she spritzes it all over as a delicious palate cleanser we both enjoy.”

Key notes: Mango, jasmine, incense, vanilla, sandalwood

ELLE editor review: “This sits at my desk and becomes an instant compliment magnet when I spray it on myself and wait for its aroma to permeate our office corner. It’s sexy and mysterious, but still inviting. While I don’t typically like to prescribe to day or night scents, this one is for the nocturnal lovers.”—NP

Buckle up tight because this fragrance takes you on a ride. The yin-yang scent composition unfurls a delicate composition of sensual citrus and florals, then comes the dip—warm almond milk, sandalwood, and white musk.

Key notes: Calypso orchid, mandarin, Bulgarian rose, almond milk, ylang ylang, iris, white musks, sandalwood

ELLE editor review: “As someone with over 100 fragrances, my signature scent changes daily. However, I almost always begin most of my scent layers with Yin Transformation, which bursts with creamy florals that feel like a balmy spring morning.”—NP

S.S.K Labs makes scent layering easier with its kits. Am-Edp is the brand's amber star, and this kit features perfume rollerball oils of myrrh and tonka bean, two notes that pair exceptionally well with amber. You can wear each on its own, or mix and match to form your own scent bubble.

Key notes: (Am) Amber, bergamot, pink pepper berry, orange blossom, musk; (Tonka) cardamom, cinnamon, nutmeg, ginger; (Myrrh) myrrh, lemongrass, cumin, amber, patchouli

Inspired by late nights partying in Paris, it’s no wonder Krigler’s Velvet Night 76 makes you want to get up and dance. If you love to mix and match your favorite scents, you’re in luck. 27 87 created their Lametta Eau De Parfum with layer-lovers in mind. Joan Tanis is the national training and events director at Maison Francis Kurkdjian.

Tynan Sinks is a New York City-based beauty writer and the co-host of fragrance podcast Smell Ya Later.

Bérénice Watteau is a fine fragrance perfumer at DSM-Firmenich

What should you consider when choosing a perfume?

Understanding fragrance notes is one of the most effective ways to find your perfect scent. “Very similar to the fashion industry, there can be trends with fragrances as well,” Tanis says. “Choosing a fragrance isn’t always easy, but make the right choice by selecting one that reflects who you are and makes you feel good. My best advice is to let your personality, tastes, and age guide your choice.”

“The most important part of finding a fragrance is, of course, trying it on,” Tanis continues, adding that you can have multiple signature scents, rather than just one, for variety. “Nowadays, it is all about a collection of fragrances. Much like clothing, modern men and women desire a fragrance wardrobe, allowing them the freedom to choose what fragrance to wear daily.”

What is the difference between an eau de toilette and eau de parfum?

Many popular scents fall into these fragrance categories. The difference between the two lies in the concentration of perfume oil each contains, says Tanis. “Eau de toilettes (EDT) have a relatively lighter composition due to their low concentration of perfume oil— between five and 15 percent. On the other hand, Eau de parfums (EDP) have a higher concentration of perfume oils—typically between 12 and 18 percent—meaning they offer a significantly stronger scent that lasts between five and eight hours.”

Choosing between the two might come down to the specific occasion or the weather (i.e., you may lean towards a lighter scent for work and during the summer months, and a richer scent for nights out and during the winter). Check out our perfume terminology guide to learn more about fragrance terms.

Tanis suggests applying fragrance to the pulse points, where the skin is naturally warm, such as the wrists, neck, or inside the elbows. “This will allow the fragrance to warm up and reveal the perfume's sillage (trail) over time.”

#6780. The 16 Best New Hotels in New York City

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

In the last five years, the Big Apple has seen some of its biggest openings and major renovations in history. These are the best of the bunch.

Visiting New York City makes for a jam-packed trip. From shows on Broadway to walks through Central Park, from exhibits at the museums (Met? MoMA? Take your pick!) to shopping Fifth Avenue, days here are booked from start to finish. Of course, it’s then that the real fun starts—Manhattan is full of the best bars and restaurants in the world.

Given the length of the to-do list, some travelers think that where they stay while in town doesn’t matter. We disagree. Just because you won’t spend the bulk of your time lounging about in your hotel robe doesn’t mean you don’t need a plush option to coddle you after an afternoon spent zipping from sight to sight or a long night out on the tiles. A big bed, crisp linens, and a killer lobby bar can make a visit, after all.

New York City is a town obsessed with the new. (It’s even right there in the name: New York City.) And over the last five years it has seen an influx of hotels that are as much the destination as the line items on any itinerary. From the namesake brands that finally have a place to call home here, like the Faena, to the most iconic renovation of the decade at the Waldorf Astoria, here is exactly where you should be booking right now.

Faena shelters you from the city’s chaos, but it’s New York through and through. To the west, views of the Hudson River Greenway and Little Island. To the east, you can high-five people on the High Line from the terrace of the Living Room bar. The most N.Y.C. touch? The staff’s suits are nicer than yours. Rooms from $1,395. —Dave Holmes

On the hotel’s website, it says that “to tell the story of New York is, in some sense, to tell the story of the Waldorf Astoria.” For once, the marketing is correct. Like Manhattan, the hotel’s story is a tale of outsize real estate ambitions. A global standard for hospitality ever since the combination of the Waldorf Hotel and the Astoria Hotel in 1897, the property became the prize in the most expensive hotel sale ever in 2014. Three years later, the new owners closed it for renovations. The project cost $2 billion and took eight years, during which time the property was traded again. The wait was worth it. From the rooms to the restaurants (there are three) to the sprawling lobby, where Cole Porter’s piano anchors the room, to the Guerlain Wellness Spa, the hotel outright dazzles. It’s the talk of Manhattan. And word is it’s already back up for sale. Rooms from $1,295. —Madison Vain

An understated facade on Thompson Street hides the sexiest hotel in SoHo. The Manner is the first of the latest brand by Standard International, and its flagship location is codesigned by the Standard’s Verena Haller and Milanese architect Hannes Peer, so the whole place is Studio 54 stylish, yet comfortable. Speakeasy Sloane’s is a great place for a cocktail and—we swear—the best chicken nuggets in town, while the Apartment is a cozy common space to use as your living room. Check out the penthouse, modeled after Halston’s office, and dream of the party you’ll throw there. There’s no better center of operations for your next New York trip. Rooms from $799. —D.H.

Don’t let yourself get swept away by the history. An Art Deco gem built in the 1920s at 76th and Madison, the Surrey was a longtime landmark in New York’s glitziest neighborhood, playing host to the likes of JFK and Bette Davis. It closed after a century and, following a multiyear renovation, recently reopened as the first North American property under global luxury hotel group Corinthia. From the outside, the property could easily pass for a residential building. Inside, you’ll find 70 rooms and 30 suites, each flawless. Muted terra-cotta-colored walls, crisp linens, and walk-in marble showers make for plush surroundings. The spa is small but divine. And the lobby bar and restaurant, Casa Tua (of Aspen, Miami, and Paris notoriety), is one of the hardest-to-get tables in town. Movie stars, politicians, famous divorcées, influencers—everyone here is someone. Including you. Rooms from $1,000. —M.V.

The Fifth Avenue Hotel leans into what New Yorkers with good taste who live in small spaces know is the secret to making their apartments feel luxurious: Love every single piece of furniture, adore every square inch. Nothing is wasted. If you’re lucky, you’ll get something like the rooms and hallways of the Fifth Avenue Hotel. It’s like rich-aunt maximalism: Missoni-style upholstered chairs and couches, an armoire that’s actually a giant bar adorned with dragons and birds. The wallpaper looks as though it’s hand-painted. Lots of tapestries and tufted pink walls. Unlike in many N.Y.C. apartments, you’ll get a great night’s sleep here—the room feels pin-drop quiet, and the bathrooms are serene marble white. This is New York, but you might not leave the hotel. There’s in-room martini service, and downstairs is one of the city’s best new restaurants, the old-school-luxe Café Carmellini, as well as the Portrait Bar, home to some of the town’s best martinis (outside of your room). Rooms from $895 —K.S.

Hotels where you pay by the month can be dicier than a Vegas craps table. But Maison Hudson is the polar opposite. Located on the banks of the Hudson and at the fringe of the West Village, the property straddles a blurry line between ultra-luxurious hotel and supremely plush apartment. The minimum stay is a month, and the price starts at thirty g’s. Sounds absurdly high? Maybe. But when suites in N.Y.C. go for several thousand dollars per night, a stay at Maison Hudson can feel kind of like a good deal over time. Boy math! Okay, so say you hit it big on a stock investment/crypto/inheritance and want to live like a 0.01 percenter in New York’s coolest neighborhood. Here’s what you get: a chic, tastefully decorated, ridiculously massive thousand-square-foot living space, complete with heated bathroom floors, automatic shades, a fully stocked kitchen, and an in-unit washer/dryer. The concierge—who is really like your own personal assistant—will get you anything you could possibly desire. (Think concert tickets, a rare bottle of wine, an endangered animal to eat for dinner—kidding!) There’s an in-house spa that offers massages, facials, and antiaging treatments in a Zen-like setting. Marius, the French-inspired restaurant helmed by Michelin-star chef Sébastien Sanjou, feels intimate with only a handful of tables. While the eatery is open to the public, don’t worry: Maison Hudson guests get first priority for seatings, natch. Rooms from $32,500 per month —Daniel Dumas

The brand’s three Manhattan locations have long been the smart traveler’s pick for affordable city lodging, and now, like so many city people, the Arlo group has made the jump into Brooklyn. On the site of the former Hotel Williamsburg, the most eye-catching of the luxury hotels to hit the hip ’hood in the 2010s, Arlo Williamsburg is the exact right home base for a New York City getaway. Brooklyn buzzes outside each room’s floor-to-ceiling windows, while a rooftop pool and a bar and event space inside the Arlo’s landmark water tower offer views of the full sweep of Manhattan’s skyline. It seems so close you could touch it, but with all of Billyburg’s bars, restaurants, shops, and general people-watching just steps from the front door, you may decide to skip the quick trip on the L and stay local. The company’s focus on art and interesting programming has survived the trip across the river. And out here you can stand in the middle of your room, extend both arms, and not touch a wall. Try that in Manhattan. Rooms from $400 —D.H.

On one end of the travel-fantasy spectrum, there is the blissed-out, blue-skies-and-beaches vacation. The stuff postcards are made of. Nothing wrong with seeking that. Toward the other end is the kind where you imagine yourself as a neighborhood local. You tap into the rhythm of the vibrant daily life in the blocks around you and feel as if you’ve merged with a hipper, alternate-universe version of yourself. That’s what Nine Orchard will do for you.

We know the “hotels for locals” shtick, where there’s a lobby filled with folks on laptops. This is not that. Nine Orchard is more about creating the fantasy of residence. It starts with your room number. 4L, 3G, 8A. You might think you’ve stepped into someone’s apartment. Each room has an appreciation for the analog—the handmade ceramic lamps, the paintings from local artists, the wooden Ojas speakers that tune in to stations curated by DJ Stretch Armstrong and Devon Turnbull. You’ll want to hole up and write a memoir here.

Lying in a cloudlike king bed, facing downtown, with One World Trade Center glimmering in the distance, I watched the sunset, thirty-four stories in the sky, and thought, What a thing to experience in a city where you thought you’d seen it all. As a gleaming, fifty-story glass tower, the Ritz-Carlton New York NoMad offers that Ritz-Carlton tranquility, but the bars and restaurants here have made it an unlikely nightlife destination in a decidedly non-touristy hood. You’ll have a sanctuary in your room with a tub—rare in N.Y.C.—but there’s also a buzzy lobby bar, the José Andrés rooftop lounge Nubeluz as well as his Bazaar Meat on the second floor, the first-floor Mediterranean delight Zaytinya, and, in the summer, the vibrant fifth-floor terrace bar, Dahlia’s Garden. It’s an uncommon New York hotel combo, where you can be serene or all-out energetic. Rooms from approx. $1,500 —K.S.

You can’t talk about the Hotel Chelsea without checking off the names of its famous tenants and guests. The building, finished in 1884, was conceived as a kind of commune for musicians and novelists and has had a bohemian artistic energy since then. Mark Twain, Stanley Kubrick, Patti Smith, Andy Warhol, and Jimi Hendrix are just some of the notable folks who have graced this Queen Anne–style building over the past century. Today it might lack a raucous clientele, but that’s all for a better night’s sleep. Expect laid-back service, marble-and-brass bathrooms, animal-print furniture. From the pitch-perfect El Quijote, a Spanish restaurant that opened in 1930, to the iconic stained-glass windows and wrought-iron balconies, checking into the Chelsea feels like entering a time warp. Rooms from approx. $300 —K.S.

Want to see something beautiful? Come stand on the corner of Wall Street and Water in Lower Manhattan and stare at this gorgeous mass of concrete. The Paspaley family, Australians who were pioneers in the pearl industry, lovingly and painstakingly brought the beaux arts facade of the Wall Street Hotel to life over the course of several years. And what’s inside is even better. Spacious suites (for Manhattan!), oversize bathrooms (see previous parenthetical), a chic-meets-fun bar that draws locals and guests, plus an absolutely killer in-house French brasserie—this place doesn’t miss. Rooms from $465 —M.V.

You’ll quickly lose track of how many food and beverage options there are at the iconic London brand’s NoMad location. Rooftop bar, lobby bar, library (with bar service), a trendy Italian spot, a private table in a water tower, and a formal dining room for table-side service. All have their own vibe and are worthy of inspection, but only some are available to hotel guests. The rest are reserved for Ned members. But worry not; there’s plenty of fun to be had for those who book here. Rooms from $375 —M.V.

The committed traveler, it stands to reason, always looks for authentic local color. So a stay in New York’s TriBeCa neighborhood should naturally involve a few nights in a soaring, modernist hymn to the twenty-four-hour possibilities of twenty-first-century New York. Right? Non. Hotel Barrière Fouquet’s, which opened recently on Greenwich Street, whiffs unmistakably and quite charmingly of Paris. For more than a hundred years, Fouquet’s existed as one of the grandest of all Parisian café-restaurants. In 2006, it entered the hotel business when it was reopened with more than one hundred luxury rooms added by the Barrière Group above and around the original café. The New York hotel is the first U.S. venture of the group, which maintains properties in some of the most chichi resorts in Europe. Its inherent Frenchness is both obvious and lightly done. The staff offer a singsongy “Bonjour” when you pass them in the corridor. In the rooms, the furnishings are postmodern, curvy takes on deco, and the toile de Jouy–style wallpaper is about as French as you can get in room decor, until you stand up close and realize it’s actually composed entirely of cartoony New York street scenes. With a relatively low room count—just ninety-seven in total, including a two-story Grand Appartement Terrasse—the hotel feels quiet and intimate. There’s just a single low-key bar, the Brasserie Fouquet’s New York, and the Par Ici Café, which doubles as the breakfast spot and where, reassuringly, the prices have been imported from Paris along with the style. Rooms from $900 —Nick Sullivan

As with many Aces around the globe, the Brooklyn outpost is very much about building a cool community hangout the only way the Ace can. The purpose-built, brutalist structure of concrete and wood is home to a lobby with a buzzing bar anchoring all of the loungy furniture, where folks Zoom on laptops by day and old-fashioneds are being stirred come nightfall. Each month features different DJs spinning soul, and the Atrium on the second floor often hosts poetry readings and film screenings. The woodshop-chic rooms aren’t bad either. Rooms from $199 —Omar Mamoon

Welcome to la dolce vita, a place where Loro Piana cashmere lines the walls of your presidential suite, where custom scents fill the air, where fireplaces roar and Bellinis are handed out by waiters in white jackets. The first of several membership clubs/boutique hotels planned by Italian hospitality’s best-known ambassadors, Casa Cipriani—located on South Street in Lower Manhattan—has had glitzy New Yorkers jockeying for access ever since it opened in 2022. The pitch was a gamble (there’s a dress code, and prices are astronomical), but it turns out that a lot of luxury feels just right these days. Rooms from approx. $750 —M.V.

Beyond the modern extension of Penn Station, you’ll come across an undulating tower seemingly conjured out of nowhere in an area that was, just a few years ago, a kind of no-man’s-land. For the trek across Ninth Avenue, the Pendry epitomizes Cali-luxe: plush, earth-toned rooms where curved windows create nooks to dwell in and admire the new urban vista. Downstairs, it’s time for a mini bar crawl. Bar Pendry is a gold-leaf-adorned jewel box for a more clandestine drink, and Chez Zou is like a belle epoque garden complete with deft cocktails and a secret cheeseburger. Rooms from $725 —K.S.

#6779. Kraft Mac & Cheese Is Taking A Big Swing With 2 Unexpected New Flavors

The latest additions are taking the brand in very different directions.

Delish editors handpick every product we feature. We may earn commission from the links on this page.

I'm as adventurous as the next person when it comes to food, but I firmly believe Kraft Mac & Cheese is one of those things that should remain as classic as classic gets. I can’t say I’ve ever felt the need to question a perfectly good bowl of cheesy pasta.

But the mac and cheese aisle is a competitive place, and sometimes you have to bring out the big guns to stand out. Apparently, Kraft found its latest ammunition somewhere between the spice cabinet and the bakery aisle.

The company is introducing two new limited-time Kraft Mac & Cheese flavors: Chili Crisp and Cinnamon Roll—and yes, you read that second one correctly. Kraft is bringing cinnamon into the cheese sauce for a combination that's supposed to be creamy, a little tangy, and reminiscent of the beloved pastry.

Chili Crisp, on the other hand, is something I can absolutely get behind. People have been adding hot sauce to mac and cheese forever, so giving the familiar blue-box dinner a little extra heat feels like a natural thing to do.

Of course, Kraft hasn't exactly been playing it safe with its flavors lately. There was Pizza Mac & Cheese, along with Jalapeño, Ranch, and Smoky Barbecue. And who could forget last year's Apple Pie Mac & Cheese, which had plenty of people questioning whether dessert and dinner really needed to share a bowl.

But even with that track record, the latest announcement has people feeling all sorts of ways.

"Yall are out of your minds," one person declared, while someone else was apparently ready to try both, writing, "Uhhhh 👁️👄👁️ I need these in my life." Even SpaghettiOs got involved, chiming in with "Honestly, I’m down 🔥".

Chili Crisp seemed to have an easier time winning people over. "Chili crisp actually 100% yea!" one person commented. And at least one fan was willing to give the more questionable Cinnamon Roll flavor a chance, thanks to a surprisingly positive experience with Kraft's previous dessert experiment.

"Everyone thought the apple pie one a year or two ago was weird, but it honestly was pretty good. I’ll give cinnamon roll a shot! 🤷‍♂️ (Chili crisp sounds amazing)"

Meanwhile, some people were less concerned with what Kraft was introducing and more interested in what the company had taken away.

"please bring back garlic parmesean, that was my favorite and i miss it dearly!!" one person pleaded. Another wanted the pizza version back, writing, "I miss your pizza one, it needs to come back." And apparently, the Alfredo fans haven't forgotten, either. "Alfredo bring back!!!" someone demanded.

For anyone ready to put their taste buds to the test, Chili Crisp is available for a limited time at Meijer, Walmart, Amazon, and other retailers, while Cinnamon Roll can be found at Walmart. Kraft hasn't shared exactly how long either will stick around, but the company is already teasing more flavors for 2027.

#6777. Giblet Gravy

Giblets may not be your go-to ingredient just yet, but this Thanksgiving, they just might steal the show. Giblets are the edible offal meat of the turkey, typically the liver, heart, gizzards, and neck, that come in a small paper or plastic pouch stuffed inside the cavity of the bird. Adding them to your traditional gravy brings a depth of flavor that takes this classic side to the next level.

This gravy will also make your life a lot easier during Thanksgiving Day—you can make it while the turkey is still in the oven. It also means you'll be able to use every part of the turkey, and you don't have to throw out that little plastic bag that comes along with your bird. We love a zero-waste recipe!

Start by removing the liver from the giblets and chilling in the refrigerator until you are ready to use.

In a large saucepan over medium heat, combine the rest of the giblets, neck, onion, carrot, garlic cloves, sage, thyme, rosemary, and broth. Bring the mixture to a boil, then reduce heat, cover, and let simmer very gently for 1 ½ hours.

Add the chilled liver to the broth and simmer until cooked through, which should take an additional 25 minutes.

Strain the broth and discard the remaining neck and all veggies. You should have about 2 ½ cups stock at this point. Afterward, finely chop all giblets and set them aside.

Melt butter in another large saucepan over medium heat. Slowly whisk in the flour and cook until bubbling and just beginning to turn golden, which should take approximately 2 to 3 minutes.

Whisk in ½ cup of strained giblet stock into the gravy at a time, waiting for the ingredient to fully incorporate into the mixture before adding any more.

Once all stock is in, let the gravy simmer while mixing constantly and scraping the bottom of the pot until thickened. This step should take approximately five minutes. Season with salt and pepper and add chopped giblets back into the pot. You’re now ready to serve!

The full list of ingredients and instructions can be found in the recipe below.

This gravy can be smothered on turkey, spooned over stuffing and mashed potatoes, or any roasted veggies, but if you have any left over (though we doubt you will), this is the perfect sauce for a leftovers sandwich or for a chicken dinner a couple of days later. Store in an airtight container in the fridge for up to 1 week.
