#6751. We’d Kill to See These MobLand Spin-Offs

With season 2 racing toward its finale and season 3 still some way off, we put our imagination to work.

If ever there were a case for “if something’s worth doing, it’s worth overdoing,” it’s MobLand. I don’t know who first said it—someone impersonating Guy Ritchie, probably.

Of all Ritchie’s gangland offspring, MobLand sits at the very tip—the tip of the iceberg, tip of the tongue, anonymous tip. It has already been tipped for awards this season.

Season 3 is understood to be in production, due sometime in late 2027. But as the second outing hurtles towards its finale this month, the wait looks positively Homeric. So, in the spirit of overdoing, here are a few spin-offs nobody asked for but richly deserve.

Newgate, the early 2000s. Kevin is a broken young man after an assault by a mean prison guard called Rusby (remember him? Kevin kills him in revenge in MobLand season 1), and he is one bad night from hanging himself with a bedsheet. His cellmate Harry, a quiet new arrival with a thousand-yard stare, talks him down. Then he does something tougher and kinder: he decides Kevin is going to live, whether Kevin likes it or not.

What follows is a double act forged in a concrete box. Harry fights, bargains and bluffs his way up the wing’s pecking order, while Kevin learns to be a person again, ultimately becoming his gobby, hapless sidekick. Between the long, dark stretches in their cell come the scams, the contraband and the beatings. A snooker table in the rec room becomes a secret casino; a rival lag turns out to be a secret snitch; a posh fraudster inmate teaches Harry to talk like an Etonian. Kevin finds his voice as a late-night prison-radio DJ.

Slowly, we watch Harry being hammered into the man that Conrad will later admire. By the final episode, he’s out, and ready to join the Paras.

I’ve made it no secret that Frankie Cooper is my favorite TV villain of 2026. But I’ll only accept a spin-off if Johnny Flynn stays in the role. So…

South London, the 1990s. Young Frankie Cooper is a gofer at a scrapyard, a well-manicured apprentice crook with an unfashionable hunger: he wants to know things. He enrolls in night classes, haunts the mobile library, and devours encyclopedias. Every lesson becomes a weapon. A lecture on Napoleon teaches him tactics. A documentary on parasites teaches him patience. Sun Tzu teaches him war.

Meanwhile he assembles his crew from the yard’s misfits: a weeping mechanic, a seven-foot pigeon fancier and a man who speaks solely in historical metaphors. Each week Frankie cons a bigger fish, charming the mark and leaving a trail of crushed-car cubes dripping blood. Beneath the bonhomie, we watch a very amiable monster being assembled, limb by limb, lesson by lesson, murder by murder.

Between all this, there's a love story—he meets the woman who will become Michael's mother. She's a nail technician from the posh part of Sydenham. She teaches him cuticle care, foot hygiene. Then she’s killed by a stray bullet in a shootout, leaving Frankie with a newborn son and a thirst for vengeance.

By the finale, the scrap yard is an empire. Then comes the one subject he can’t revise for: single fatherhood.

Ten years after MobLand, the Harrigans are all dead. Kat MacAllister won the war, salted the earth, and walked into the White House as president of the United States. But she never forgot that Harry turned her down. He survived the war, and lives off-grid on a remote Scottish isle, tending sheep and reading Plato. Once a month, he gets a postcard from Gina who is studying a master’s degree in Comparative Criminal Justice at The Hague.

One day, Harry receives a strange postcard from Gina, asking him to come. But something isn’t right. She signs off with “Your hugglepuff, Georgina.” He’s never called her Hugglepuff, and she always hated the name Georgina.

It’s a trap, and Harry knows it. Still, he dusts down his old leather coat and gets a ferry to the mainland. What follows is a race around Europe, from the Hebrides to Belgium to Washington DC, on one last job to save the only person he ever truly loved. On the journey he discovers that Gina’s criminology thesis accidentally unearthed a paper trail linking Kat's old MobLand war chest to the untraceable offshore funds that financed her presidential campaign.

Kat sees an opportunity. If she baits Harry out of the woodwork, she can frame him for a rogue, violent operation in Europe, eliminating both the evidence (Gina) and the last loose end from her past (Harry) in one clean stroke.

The hound is out of retirement. And, this time, it’s presidential.

This one charts Maeve’s humble beginnings in the wild west of Ireland. It follows her early years picking the pockets of drunk farmers at the Galway Races. At a dance, an amorous young man drags Maeve into a bush. What he hasn't considered is the razor blade she keeps in her petticoat. She cuts off his testicles.

She's the youngest and smallest–and by far the smartest—of a clan of bare-knuckle boxer brothers who never think to ask her to a fight.

So she watches and learns. She learns what men do when they think no one is watching, and what they’ll pay to keep it quiet. Her first scheme is small and sly. Her tenth ends with a bookie in a bog and the Irish mob on her tail.

She heads to London where she meets a young pickpocket with a bad mustache and a worse temper, and she sees the perfect partner. His name is Conrad Harrigan. Together they murder every last gangster with a grudge (and there are many).

It’s a love story, of a kind. Two outlaws, one getaway car, and a trail of widows in their rearview mirror.

#6749. Spoilers: Where Erika and Blake Stand Now After Breaking Things Off During ‘Love Is Blind’

Reminder: This season was filmed a year ago...things change!

Erika and Blake genuinely seemed to form a connection during Love Is Blind, and honestly...we had hope that they’d make it down the aisle. Sure, they had vastly different lifestyles (he literally lives on a boat for most of the year), but they seemed like soulmate material, and Blake was...kinda willing (?) to settle down and think about starting a family. Buuuut things started getting complicated after Blake’s close friend refused to support the relationship, and ultimately the season ended with Erika ending the relationship ahead of their wedding.

“I think at this point there’s too many concerns, too many questions, that I can’t say yes at the altar,” Erika said, adding, “I think the best thing to do is just to leave the experiment.” Cut to Blake talking to a producer and saying “I’m feeling a little relieved, a little exhausted. Erika cried, I didn’t cry. Who said I cried? I didn’t cry! Erika cries a bunch...you know. It was sad.”

So, where do things stand now? Love Is Blind recently filmed its season 11 reunion, and thanks to an early viewing for fans on Tudum, we know they are no longer together (in fact, they’ve both dated other people since their split), but they definitely had a vibe. Which makes sense; their breakup was reluctant and more to do with physical distance/lifestyle than anything else. Either way, Erika teased “if he wants me there he’ll find a way to get me there” when asked about traveling with Blake, just saying!

Sounds like romance isn’t completely dead for these two, so stay tuned.

#6748. ELLE Escapes: Palm Beach

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

There’s just something about Palm Beach. Maybe it’s the namesake trees, the prime people-watching, or the air of exclusivity that comes with its private beaches and members-only clubs. Whatever the draw, this laid-back island feels more desirable than ever, even if a presidential visit to Mar-a-Lago means contending with a little extra gridlock. Roll down the windows, leave reality behind, and settle into one of Florida’s most rarefied corners for a weekend of luxury, indulgence, and enviable living. Ahead, I’ve rounded up where the well-heeled snowbirds eat, drink, and shop on their next trip down south.

With shops like Zimmermann, Hermès, and Veronica Beard, a tempting lineup of restaurants, and even a gelato stand at Sant Ambreous, the John Volk-designed Royal Poinciana Plaza is more than enough to fill an afternoon, and you’ll be happy to let it.

Golf and Palm Beach go hand in hand. If you’re a novice like me, this scenic par-3 course overlooking the water is the ideal place to work on your swing—or relive those regrettable bogeys long after you’ve left the green.

Step into the spa at Eau Resort & Spa, and it’s immediately clear why it earned a place in the hotel’s name. From the impeccable service and indulgent treatments to the top-tier facilities, the sprawling 42,000-square-foot space is the ultimate place for a little (or a lot of) pampering.

Lack of Color Utopia Inca Raffia Bucket Hat

Does it get any better than Nobu? From its signature miso black cod and rock shrimp tempura to the yellowtail jalapeño and specialty rolls, every dish is a standout. Add impeccable presentation to the mix, and this Japanese-Peruvian favorite never disappoints. Its new location at the Eau is no exception.

Set on the picturesque terrace of The Brazilian Court Hotel, this much-talked-about Daniel Boulud-helmed restaurant lives up to the hype. You really can’t go wrong with anything on the menu, but the White Cosmo (a mix of vodka, elderflower, and white cranberry, finished with an orchid ice cube) is begging to be Instagrammed.

For the uninitiated, the Nora (North of the Railroad) District in West Palm Beach is quickly becoming the city’s next big thing, and Del Mar is helping lead the charge. Steps away from the newly opened Nora Hotel, its Mediterranean-inspired menu and sprawling 12,000-square-foot space, complete with a 100-seat courtyard, feel both inviting and refined. Don’t miss the Open Fire Roasted Shellfish Tower, made for sharing (though I won’t judge if you keep it all to yourself).

Farm Rio Golden Yellow Anthurium Square Neck Mini Dress

Alexis Bittar Liquid Lucite Front Back Hoop Earring

Andrea Gomez Bella Raffia Platform Sandals

Open for breakfast, lunch, or dinner, Swifty’s, the poolside restaurant at The Colony Hotel, proves that casual dining can still deliver. Stick around for the live music, particularly the acoustic guitarists who stick to the hits (no requests necessary). And before you call it a day, make a pit-stop at the pool-deck newsstand to pick up the latest issue of ELLE.

HMF, the restaurant and lounge at The Breakers, is a great spot for a pre-dinner drink before wandering the resort’s storied halls. And its wine credentials are nothing short of legendary: It’s earned a Grand Award from Wine Spectator every year since the awards program began in 1981.

Just a block from the ocean, this picturesque, pink-hued property packs plenty into its 79 rooms, including a sunset-facing pool deck, a gym, a ballroom, and, of course, a stunning bar.

Coniglio Palm Beach Cinch Back Silk Midi Skirt

Tucked away on Lake Mangonia in West Palm Beach, the Belgrove Resort & Spa has all the makings of a perfect getaway. Think an 18-hole golf course, four tennis courts, two pickleball courts, two padel courts, private balconies in every guest room, a gym, a spa, three pools, and two hot tubs. Whether you’re after an action-packed escape or some serious R&R, you’ll find it here. And when you’re ready to venture out, a complimentary shuttle will take you anywhere within a five-mile radius.

Sizable suites? Check. A private, shaded pool and on-site hair salon? Check. Custom Linus bikes to borrow and complimentary yacht rides? Absolutely. The Brazilian Court Hotel feels like a true home base in the heart of town, just a quick ride from the Palm Beach Lake Trail. As owner Courtney Schlesinger puts it: “The hotel was built to feel like a private estate, so when you’re here, you feel like you have your own place in Palm Beach. That’s what I love the most: waking up in the morning, sitting on the terrace with a coffee, and imagining Daniel Boulud is my own private chef.”

A little off the beaten path and about a 20-minute drive from Palm Beach, Eau Resort & Spa more than makes up for its location with direct beach access, guest rooms designed by Jonathan Adler, two pools, a world-class spa, and a full-service beauty salon offering manicures, pedicures, and blowouts. Add six restaurants (including Nobu) and kid-friendly perks like childcare and an arcade, and you have everything you need for a getaway without ever leaving the property. So why would you?

The Row Ginza Leather and Suede Platform Flip Flops

For a face that gleams in the sunlight, pack this hydrating oil with vitamin C in your beach bag.

L’Oréal Paris Voluminous Lash Paradise Waterproof Mascara

If you plan jumping in the pool, applying waterproof mascara is a must. Sweat- and water-resistant with a lengthening effect, this one pairs well with a cocktail.

Shiseido Urban Environment Oil-Free Sunscreen SPF 42

Just because you’re longing for a tan doesn’t mean you can skimp on the SPF. This Shiseido sunscreen is sheer and blendable, with a matte yet hydrating finish. I swear by it!

#6747. Here’s Where Wilnie and Wilson Stand Now After Breaking Up on ‘Love Is Blind’

Attention everyone wondering if they got back together….

Not gonna sugarcoat it: This wasn’t a very successful season of Love Is Blind. Multiple couples broke up either at the altar or before making it there at all, including Wilnie and Wilson. We had high hopes for these two, but things changed after their respective bachelor and bachelorette parties, and they ultimately decided to part ways. The issue? Wilson thinking Wilnie should do more in the way of household responsibilities (including…cooking for him) and wanting her to be more “fun.”

Meanwhile, Wilnie said, “I don’t think you really like me, and I’ve been thinking you’re not going to say yes at the altar.” She also told producers, “He expected me to be more like his mom,” while Wilson’s parting words were essentially “grow up, dude.”

Of course, we can’t forget that Love Is Blind season 11 was filmed over a year ago (if fan sleuthing is to be believed), and a lot can change in that time. But the cast just got back together for a reunion, where they updated everyone on where things stand. And we have intel!

Reality Receipts podcaster Rikkii Wise (@storytimewithrikkii) dropped a slew of spoilers and revealed that Wilnie and Wilson are not back together, which was confirmed during an early fan viewing of the reunion on Saturday.

Sorry to everyone hoping they’d work it out! The good news, though? They appear to be fast friends, and considering they both live in the Boston area, there’s always a chance at reconciliation going forward? As Wilson put it, “Me and Wilnie, we check in on each other. More recently…maybe not?”

#6746. Nobody Wants This Season 4: Everything We Know

Creator Erin Foster has weighed in on the show’s future.

Spoilers for Nobody Wants This season 3 below.

Three seasons into Nobody Wants This, Joanne (Kristen Bell) and Noah (Adam Brody) have finally reached the altar—or, more accurately, a Malibu beach. But does their wedding also mark the end of the series?

The third season, which premiered on Netflix on October 22, sees the couple scrap their carefully planned nuptials and exchange vows shortly after Joanne completes her conversion to Judaism.

It’s a significant development for a couple whose differences have driven much of the series since its 2024 debut. But while Joanne and Noah have settled one major question about their future, plenty of others remain unanswered. Morgan (Justine Lupe), Sasha (Timothy Simons), and Esther (Jackie Tohn), in particular, have some unfinished business.

So, does the season 3 finale mark the end of Nobody Wants This, or is there another chapter ahead? Here’s everything we know about a potential fourth season.

On November 6, Netflix confirmed that Nobody Wants This will return for a fourth season. “We are so happy and thrilled to do another season of our favorite show on television, which coincidentally is the one we run,” co-showrunners and executive producers Erin Foster, Jenni Konner, and Bruce Eric Kaplan said in a joint statement.

Prior to the announcement, Foster expressed interest in continuing the story. In an October 23 interview with Parade, Foster discussed the possibilities for Joanne and Noah after their wedding, drawing on her own experience of getting married at 37.

“There’s those next steps as people who are not in their twenties, getting married and you’re starting off already kind of behind everyone else,” she said at the time. “I experienced that when I got married; I was 37, and it’s a different experience. So, yeah, I do think that there’s more story to tell.” Foster added, “I never want to have a show that goes on longer than people want to see it. I’d rather people want more than want less.”

The third season gives Joanne and Noah a relatively conclusive ending, but their wedding also introduces a different stage of their relationship.

After getting engaged in episode 4, the couple spends much of the season trying to plan a ceremony that accommodates their families, particularly Noah’s opinionated mother, Bina (Tovah Feldshuh). Eventually, they decide to marry on their own terms, exchanging vows on the beach after Joanne’s mikvah, the ritual immersion that completes her conversion to Judaism.

Their marriage could provide the starting point for season 4, especially given Foster’s comments about the challenges of starting a family later in life. The show has already introduced the possibility of children for Joanne and Noah, although no future storyline has been confirmed.

There is also the matter of their families finding out about the wedding. In her Parade interview, Foster suggested that Bina would be particularly upset to discover that the ceremony she had spent so much time organizing happened without her.

Then there’s the considerably more complicated situation between Morgan, Sasha, and Esther.

After separating from Esther at the end of season 2, Sasha grows closer to Morgan, and the two eventually sleep together during a trip to New York. But their potential romance stalls when Sasha admits he isn’t ready for another serious relationship. He subsequently reconnects with Esther, and the former spouses sleep together as well.

By the finale, both women know about Sasha’s involvement with the other, leaving the three characters without a clear resolution.

Ahead of the season 3 premiere, Tohn spoke to ELLE about Esther’s relationship with Sasha and whether the two might eventually reconcile.

“I think Sasha and Esther get back together, but I have no idea. I’m not in the writers’ room,” she said.

Tohn also acknowledged that audiences are divided over whom Sasha should end up with, having seen fans advocate for both Esther and Morgan.

For her part, Lupe has welcomed the growing interest in Morgan and Sasha’s relationship. In an October 22 interview with WWD, the actress discussed why she thinks viewers have connected with the unlikely pairing.

“I think people gravitate toward things that are a little bit more strange sometimes, and it’s nice to be representing the black sheep or the underdogs or the loser siblings,” she said.

Whether Sasha ultimately pursues Morgan, reunites with Esther, or chooses neither remains to be seen. But unlike Joanne and Noah’s marriage, that storyline has yet to reach a definitive conclusion.

Netflix has not announced a release date or production timeline for season 4, but if the next installment follows the show’s release trends, it could likely arrive in fall of 2027. Season 2 debuted on Oct. 23, 2025, and season 3 arrived on Oct. 22, 2026.

All three seasons of Nobody Wants This are currently streaming on Netflix.

#6745. Are Aneha and Cam Back Together After Ending Things on ‘Love Is Blind’? Where They Stand Now

Aneha and Cam had a lot of issues throughout their Love Is Blind journey, the biggest being that her parents had no interest in (a) meeting Cam or (b) attending their wedding. But on top of that hurdle, the couple weren’t exactly communicating their feelings well, and had very different ideas about where to live and how to manage their work/life balance. So it didn’t exactly come as a shock when Aneha turned Cam down at the altar, saying “I still believe you’re my person but I do not [say yes] today. I need more time.”

Despite saying “I don’t,” Aneha seemed very open to dating Cam and working on their relationship, even telling producers “I can’t imagine my life without him.” Meanwhile, the pair had a whole convo about taking time to process where they stand and their continued love for each other, which seemed promising. But…did they stay together?

Not so much. Love Is Blind was allegedly filmed over a year ago, and according to the reunion (which was filmed recently), they are not together. Aneha revealed they’d planned a hotel retreat for after their wedding, and that during their time away Cam told her he didn’t love her and wanted to end the relationship.

Cam also revealed he was planning to say no to Aneha on their wedding day; she just happened to go first. So, guess that’s that on that! But in better news, they seem to be getting along fine as friends?

#6744. 13 Best Deodorant for Men 2026, According to Dermatologists

Frankly, the best deodorant for men sells itself. It stinks to stink: Body odor is right up there with bad breath as a totally avoidable misfortune. But while deodorant might seem like an odd category to get overly psyched about, as a longtime grooming editor, I can confidently say we're living in a golden age of the underarm essential.

Unlike the synthetic and chalky ghosts of deodorant sticks past, today’s best deodorants are a a sea of sophisticated formulas, improved textures, better scents, and naturally derived ingredients that, yes, actually work. The only hard part is honing in on the best one for you. Price, feel, skin sensitivity, and your sweat and odor levels matter. So I tested a whole lot of deodorants to narrow the field to seven standouts, some new, some tried-and-true, plus a few more deeper cuts I also loved.

I also tapped trusted dermatologists, for some expert insights further down. Just one last quick note: We’re not talking antiperspirants here, which primarily rely on aluminum compounds to reduce sweat. If that’s what you’re after, I’ve got recommendations for those, too.

Now, are there more targeted, more sophisticated, and, well, chicer deodorants on this list? Absolutely. But at the end of the day, you want your deo to do three things: keep the stink at bay, smell good, and be easy to repurchase. Dove Men+Care hits every mark, which is why it's in every drugstore.

The price, of course, is great, but then there's the scent: a surprisingly sophisticated blend of bright bergamot and warm white amber that's fresh, subtly citrusy, and softly warm. It smells so much better than your typical underarm fare and is actually a little mood-boosting, as the packaging suggests. The aluminum-free, baking-soda-free formula features moisture-attracting ingredients, giving it a little skincare credibility, too. It also feels super smooth going on, and kept its scent for most of the day. No notes!

Hip grooming brand Church California's deodorant only launched two years ago, but for me, it's everything a niche, forward-thinking deodorant should be: Effective, made with almost entirely naturally derived ingredients, and housed in compostable, plastic-free packaging. The formula uses magnesium and organic tapioca starch for odor and moisture control, with coconut oil and shea butter adding a little skin-conditioning goodness. And that scent? Wonderfully herbaceous, thanks to an essential oil blend that smells like the actual outdoors.

Admittedly, the sturdy cardboard tube takes a little getting used to—you push the stick up from the bottom instead of twisting a dial. And the glide is certainly a little less smooth. But oh, does the formula hold up. Even hours later, I'd catch little whiffs of those lovely botanicals, and when the scent eventually faded, there was still no detectable B.O. For the environmentally conscious guy, it's a no-brainer, and for the aesthete, it looks retro cool sitting on your medicine cabinet shelf.

Ursa Major’s Hoppin’ Fresh stick is a modern legend in the deo space. Among us grooming geeks, this au naturel-meets-elevated formula changed the underarm game when it launched in 2015. It was one of those early artisanal step-ups from drugstore fare, and it earned the hype with thoughtfully curated, foraged-sounding ingredients, a subtle, natural-smelling citrus scent, and a skincare-centric stick that glides on smoothly.

It still holds up today, fighting odor, soothing skin, and absorbing excess moisture to keep you smelling fresh. For an aluminum-free deo, it also keeps me pretty dry, too. And even the packaging—a sleek, solid-blue tube—feels like a style upgrade. Scent wise, in winter Forest Fix is the way to go. But the original Hoppin' Fresh is always waiting in the wings once the warmer months come around.

I'm well aware that Old Spice has plenty of newer, more fragrance-centric variations, many with names like Swagger. But I've always preferred the tried-and-true stuff, and that blue stick, here in High Endurance Refresh, is about as iconic as deodorant gets. It's my old reliable, and one I always keep as a backup, whether I'm visiting family, staying overnight somewhere, or just need something I can count on in a pinch. This classic has aged remarkably well, because it simply works.

Then there's the nostalgia, which hits me every time I catch a whiff of that distinctively fresh-meets-medicinal aroma I've loved unironically for years. The formula isn't particularly fancy: science-y ingredients give the aluminum-free stick its smooth glide, while glycerin adds a little skin-conditioning benefit. Would I wear this for an Ironman competition? Probably not. But for everyday errands, workdays, and just about everything in between? Absolutely. At under $15 for a three-pack, it's also easily one of the best value options on this list.

When you think gel deodorant, you probably picture those old-school perforated dispensers with tons of holes. But Baxter of California's is a smooth, transparent gel stick that's still, in my book, among the best. Part of the appeal is the formula, which packs some impressive botanicals: witch hazel and tea tree oil help tackle odor-causing bacteria, while chamomile helps soothe skin. Add tried-and-true glycerin to the mix, and you've got a gel deodorant with some serious skincare cred, too.

Then there's the application itself. The clear gel glides on effortlessly, with a cool, crisp, almost medicinal feel that's especially refreshing during the warmer months. Better still, it won't leave white marks on your shirts, something I always appreciate with my go-to black T-shirts. Is it the most heavy-duty odor fighter on this list? Not quite. But for everyday wear, it keeps the funk at bay without feeling like you've applied much of anything.

I test a lot of deodorants, but few become permanent fixtures in my bathroom. So it's worth telling you that Freaks of Nature's Everyday Endurance has become my primary deodorant lately. The brand may be known best for sunscreen, but it knows its way around our underarms too. And much like its sunscreens, part of the appeal is the application. The lotion-like formula emerges through a clever F-shaped grate, and there's something oddly satisfying about watching it dispense before smoothing it onto your underarms.

Better still, it absorbs well without any gunky, sticky residue. Cocoa butter and sunflower oil give it a skin-conditioning quality that makes this one of the most soothing deodorants I've ever worn. It's premium-priced, sure, but between the standout application, exemplary performance, and skin-conditioning feel, it's worth every penny.

Gone are the days when deodorant had to smell like “Sports Ice Rush.” Today's best odor fighters offer sophisticated, subtle scents (so they don't compete with your cologne), and Salt & Stone remains one of the top examples, with deodorants that have actual scent pyramids—Saffron & Cedar, Black Rose & Oud, Bergamot & Hinoki, for example. All are nuanced, refined, and never too loud.

But it's not just about the scent. The formula neutralizes odor and absorbs excess moisture very well, while skincare-centric additions like niacinamide and shea butter help keep underarms comfortable. More importantly, in my experience, the fragrance doesn't devolve into some funky olfactory murk by day's end. You still smell like the scent you applied that morning, or at the very least, fresh. Are these the heaviest-duty or most affordable deodorants out there? Nope. But they deliver on fragrance and function, and that's a combination worth investing in.

Caldera Lab just launched its first deodorant, and given the brand's skincare chops, it's no surprise the formula reads like a serum: niacinamide, aloe vera, and hyaluronic acid soothe and hydrate; postbiotics and magnesium tackle odor; and arrowroot absorbs moisture. Stacked, indeed.

Can Aesop do any wrong? Not really, and its deodorant spray is another example of the botanical-meets-aromatic goodness the amber-hued brand does so well. It's the only spray on this list, and while its odor protection holds up well, a midday refresh is sometimes warranted—and honestly, part of the pleasure.

If your skin is extra sensitive, Nécessaire's entire fragrance-free lineup is exemplary, and its deodorant stick is no exception. It keeps odor impressively in check, is easy on sensitive underarms, and won't compete with your favorite cologne. It's also aluminum- and baking soda-free, dermatologist-tested, hypoallergenic, and even carries the National Eczema Association's Seal of Acceptance.

Cremo may be kings of shave cream, but it knows a thing or two about deodorant, too. The formulas are affordable, the odor protection holds up, and the scents smell so damn good. Take Palo Santo, with its warm, woody, slightly smoky aroma that feels far more sophisticated than its price suggests. Coconut oil and shea butter add the skin-conditioning touch you'd expect from a pricier stick. rant quietly in the background, look elsewhere—but if you like a lot of fragrance with your funk-fighting, Cremo delivers serious bang for the buck.

The Grey's culty, rightfully hyped deodorant is the best old-school-style roll-on I've ever tried. The unscented formula is so lightweight you almost have to check you're applying anything, but oh, you are, and there's a cooling tingle as it goes on. It's the most expensive deodorant on this list, but with its premium slim aluminum casing and impressively lightweight formula, it's an investment in barely-there odor-fighting care.

Malin+Goetz's Eucalyptus Deodorant is a stone-cold classic that helped elevate gel sticks and make them feel refreshingly natural. The scent is practically a trademark at this point, equal parts medicinal, herbal, and oh-so-subtle. But there's substance behind it: eucalyptus extract helps refresh skin, while odor-neutralizing citronellyl methylcrotonate keeps the funk at bay.

As a grooming editor, I've tried more sticks, sprays, gels, and roll-ons than I can count, from drugstore staples to niche newcomers. And while plenty have impressed me on first swipe, the real test is whether I keep reaching for them weeks, months, or even years later.

Here, I drew on firsthand testing, focusing on odor protection, scent, texture, application, skin comfort, and, of course, how well they hold up. I've worn these deodorants through warm and cool weather, long days, and everything in between. Some are favorites I've relied on for years, while others are newer that are now part of my medicine cabinet rotation. As I've said, the best deodorant sometimes depends on the occasion or even the season, so it's a good idea to have a few on hand if possible. At the end of the day, a deodorant needs to keep B.O. at bay, ideally absorb excess moisture, and feel comfortable. Some excel in certain areas more than others, but each one above warrants its spot among the best deodorants.

Here is some dermatologist advice on deodorants—the best ingredients to look for, plus some controversial ones, too—with insights from board-certified dermatologists Kiran Mian, Ryan B. Turner, and Heather Rogers.

The primary cause of body odor is “bad” bacteria pairing up with sweat. There are two ways to prevent the stink: Reduce perspiration in the area, and/or create an inhospitable environment for the bacteria.

These are the most common ingredients to seek out if you want to temper the proliferation of bacteria in your pits (and anywhere you’re particularly pungent).

This antimicrobial and anti-inflammatory mineral safely and effectively kills bacteria without irritating skin, says Mian.

Dr. Mian also endorses the non-irritating magnesium hydroxide, a naturally occurring compound that neutralizes bacteria without absorbing into the skin.

“The terpenes in tea tree oil make this ingredient naturally antibacterial and antifungal, helping to reduce body odor,” Turner says. “In its pure, concentrated form, it can be very irritating. It’s best to use a product that’s formulated with tea tree oil at low percentages. It’s still effective when diluted, and a little bit goes a very long way.”

This one is controversial to some. Most skin types will tolerate baking soda (sodium bicarbonate) just fine, but only trust reputable, well-reviewed brands who use it. “Baking soda has a very alkaline pH level, while that of the skin is more acidic,” explains Turner. “This disrupts the skin’s natural barrier, harming the good bacteria in our microbiome, and leaving our skin more susceptible to irritation and inflammation.” (We’ve included a few deodorants with baking soda below, plus many natural picks that explicitly exclude it.)

It’s easy to get these two confused, but they share a principle. “Prebiotics feed the good bacteria on our skin (bacteria that does not break down sweat releasing a foils odor), which then overpopulates those odor-producing bacteria,” explains Mian. “Probiotics are the good bacteria that will ideally populate the skin and overtake odor causing bacteria.” The names of these might be strange to you, like lactobacillus acidophilus or saccharomyces ferment. Chances are, a brand will proudly tout their pre- and probiotics, so look for that indication instead.

“These chemical exfoliating ingredients help resurface the skin, which also strips away bacteria as well as removes the dead skin that bacteria break down, which causes body odor,” says Turner. And Dr. Rogers echoes the love: “Using acids to exfoliate the skin in our armpits helps rebuild the acidic mantle [which is compromised with age], leading to better control of bacterial growth and odor. Many acids work great; glycolic, lactic acid and mandelic acid are my favorites. Salicylic acid, a beta hydroxy acid, has a higher chance of irritation.”

“Activated charcoal can help to absorb sweat and help to neutralize odors,” says Turner.

Arrowroot powder, tapioca starch, and cornstarch are popular ingredients in aluminum-free deodorants, helping absorb excess moisture and keep underarms feeling dry without blocking sweat production. Of tapioca starch, Mian says, “This non-irritating starch won’t clog sweat ducts like aluminum, which can cause breakouts.” (It's also terrific in anti-chafe products for similar moisture-absorbing benefits.) “Like tapioca starch, corn starch pulls moisture from the skin to leave a powdery feeling behind. Unless you have an allergy to these plants, they are safer to use,” says Turner. Both doctors particularly favor arrowroot powder as a naturally derived, generally well-tolerated ingredient for absorbing moisture.

Both help soak up surface moisture without blocking sweat production. Freaks of Nature's formula includes bentonite clay.

A naturally occurring, porous mineral powder that absorbs excess moisture and helps keep underarms dry. You'll find it in Ursa Major's deodorant.

Approach your deodorant purchase with the following considerations:

While it’s easy to celebrate a fully natural formula—and for good reason—keep in mind that not all natural ingredients are inherently good for your skin. Ingredients like baking soda and certain essential oils can cause irritation, particularly if your underarms are sensitive. Conversely, synthetic ingredients aren't automatically bad for your skin, either. “What’s more important is to consider the manufacturer of the products—are they reputable? Do they perform extensive testing? What are the ingredients used and from where are they sourced?” says Turner. That's sound advice whether you're shopping natural or synthetic. There's a reason dermatologists frequently recommend established brands like Dove: They have the resources to develop, test, and manufacture formulas at scale, often at a fraction of the price of more niche alternatives. Of course, that doesn't mean smaller brands aren't doing their homework, either. Finding the right deodorant still takes some trial and error, gents, but you can always start with our list.

If your underarms are easily irritated, start with the ingredient list. Baking soda, strong fragrances, essential oils, and certain acids can trigger irritation, though everyone's skin reacts differently. Look for skin-conditioning ingredients like shea butter, glycerin, and aloe, several of which you'll find in the deodorants featured above. Fragrance-free formulas are a smart starting point for sensitive skin, while unscented are worth considering, too—just know that some contain masking fragrances.

Deodorants come in all sorts of formats these days, from classic solid sticks and clear gels to sprays, roll-ons, lotions, and pastes. How they feel when you apply them matters as much as how well they work. Some glide on more smoothly, while others feel chalkier, thicker, or more moisturizing. Gels and roll-ons tend to feel lightweight and refreshing (great for warmer weather), while richer lotions and pastes feel more nourishing and suit the cooler months. Ultimately, the best deodorant feels comfortable, doesn't leave residue on your clothes, and fits your lifestyle.

Dr. Kiran Mian is a board-certified dermatologist at Hudson Dermatology and Laser Surgery in NYC, and volunteer faculty at the Icahn School of Medicine at Mount Sinai University.

Dr. Ryan B. Turner is a board-certified dermatologist and founder of Turner Dermatology in NYC and the TRNR Skin skin care line.

Dr. Heather Rogers is a board-certified dermatologist and co-founder of Modern Dermatology in Seattle and the founder of the Doctor Rogers skin care line.

#6743. Bob Dylan Dressed Like Timothée Chalamet to Meet the Pope

Today, millions of Americans awoke to social media feeds awash with images of and commentary about a landmark event that seemed nigh-impossible a mere 24 hours earlier. I speak, of course, of this image:

As the nation began to digest the results of a historic election that seemed to herald the arrival of a new America, two of our very best Americans met for a private audience on the other side of the Atlantic: Bob Dylan, the Great American artist, and Pope Leo XIV, the first American Pope. Dylan is in the midst of yet another lengthy European tour—his strongest in some number of years, judging by recent bootleg tapes. On November 2, he played a show at Milan’s Unipol Dome, then jetted over to Rome ahead of a November 5 performance at the ​​Palazzo dello Sport. What better way to kill an off-day than a quick chat with the Vicar of Christ?

It wasn’t the first time Bob met a Pope, as some pointed out. In 1997, he performed for John Paul II at the World Eucharistic Congress in Bologna, donning a dapper Western suit and a fine Stetson for the occasion. He and JP2 made for a bit of an odd couple, the mysterious American gunslinger and the seemingly-nonplussed Polish holy man.

Dylan has somewhat more in common with Pope Leo, a.k.a Robert Francis Prevost, a fellow Midwesterner. But even if the two men hail from similar geography, the sight of them together under the bright fluorescent lights of an anonymous Vatican office looked like the product of a well-crafted AI prompt, if not an outright fever dream. The images were so unexpected, so uncanny, I would’ve been convinced they were fake had they not been published by an official press organ of the Vatican.

Much of the whiplash stemmed from the two men’s appearances. Angel and devil. Spy versus spy. The man in the long black coat and the man in the short white mozzetta. The one liners wrote themselves.

On Wednesday, the Pope wore a clean white cassock and a tidy little zucchetto, with an iron cross hanging down around his neck. In short, he looked like the Pope. I suppose I would say the same about Bob: that he looked like himself. As any good Dylan fan knows, however, the meaning of that phrase has changed radically over the years. Bob’s shifting appearance is almost as notorious as his shifting artistic sensibility. Dust bowl refugee, sharp-suited mod, bohemian poltergeist, whatever this is—the man has worn many hats.

A hoodied Timothée Chalamet, seen with Kylie Jenner, in New York City.

Sometime in the early 1990s, Dylan took a liking to the humble hoodie, a stylistic evolution that intersected neatly with his booze-fueled artistic nadir. The hoodie allowed him to hide in plain sight, even as he continued to take the stage around the world on his Never Ending Tour. It was practical, yes, but it was also a defense mechanism, a way to synthesize his compulsion to perform and his aversion to being seen. That it often looked hard as hell did not hurt. (It should come as little surprise that Timothée Chalamet, who may as well have been born wearing a hoodie, seemed to riff on this era when he was channeling Bob all those months ago.)

In the 21st century, Bob came unstuck from time. On landmark records like “Love And Theft” and Modern Times, Dylan burrowed into an imaginary musical past situated somewhere between the Civil War and the Great Depression. His style shifted to match. In the early aughts, he adopted a full country-western guise, complete with a Lee Van Cleef mustache. In the years that followed, his sartorial inspirations shifted east: a little less cowboy, a little more riverboat gambler, often performing in wide-brimmed hats, long frock coats, and string ties. “Rock and roll Mark Twain” wouldn’t have been an inaccurate descriptor.

Which brings us back to today’s meeting with Pope Leo. On first glance, Dylan’s ensemble is bonkers: western black blouson with contrast stitching along the chest and collar; beneath that, a heavyweight black hoodie zipped to the chest; beneath that, a white collared shirt with several buttons undone, collar loosely drawn together with a polka-dot cravat. The hood is decidedly up, stationed atop a black flat brim cap. In some shots, Bob can even be seen sporting leather gloves and an old-fashioned umbrella, which seems to double as a cane.

Part “Man in Black” sobriety, part rock star debauchery, with a dash of the hypebeast flavor from Timmy’s A Complete Unknown press tour—the man got a damn fit off in Vatican City. There’s no other way to describe it. (It’s a shame Pope Leo left his Rhude jacket back at the Papal Palace.)

Still, it’s a look with roots in Bob’s past, or pasts: the hoodies of the ’90s, the Westernwear of the 2000s, even the polka dots of 1965. No one but Bob would ever dare to pair all these disparate elements, particularly for a private audience with the Bishop of Rome. No one but Bob could.

Someone should come up with a word for that.

#6742. ‘Love Is Blind’ Reunion Reveals Brand-New Relationship and Very Dramatic Breakup That Took Place Entirely Off-Camera

As anyone keeping up with the chaos that is Love Is Blind’s Boston era knows, Rob and Michaella did…not work out. Basically, Rob had a meltdown during a cast reunion at a local bar, told everyone he didn’t care about their relationship, pointed out they’d only known each other for a few weeks, flipped out over Michaella talking to beekeeper Matt, and then ditched Michaella in the middle of the night.

However! If your takeaway from the bar night was that Michaella and Matt still had unresolved chemistry, you are not alone. Thanks to the reunion, we know that Michaella and Matt started dating after filming wrapped on Love Is Blind. But they didn’t last, and honestly it was extremely awkward.

Matt seemed to think he and Michaella were in love and had a “serious” relationship, whereas her point of view was more: “Matt and I had a really great friendship that shouldn't have crossed friendship—we dated for a few weeks.” She also added, “I don’t think we were in love?” and claimed she only dropped the L-word when she was on mushrooms. Cut to Matt, who passive-aggressively informed everyone he was glad he didn’t bring her honey. Which, okay!

As for where Michaella and Rob stand, they are absolutely not together. Michaella recently told Tudum, “Him behaving that way just felt like such a betrayal to me.” She added, “While he’s doing it, I’m sitting there thinking to myself, ‘What are you doing? You just met my parents. I have shown you the most I’ve ever shown anybody about myself and been so patient and kind. How could you be saying these things in front of your friends and on camera?’”

Michaella added that she wishes they’d left the show together, saying, “It all just got to a really dark place. I think it would’ve been better for his mental state. He was so stressed, and I don’t want anyone to be stressed.”

#6741. Kate Middleton Gave a Rare Look at Her Natural Hair During a Charity Visit

Kate Middleton is known for her polished hairstyles during royal engagements, be it elegant curls, updos, or voluminous ponytails. But the Princess of Wales embraced her natural wavy hair texture during a secret visit to Dose of Nature on October 21, according to Hello! and InStyle. Kate shared photos from the visit on Instagram, along with a reflection on her time at the nature-focused mental health charity.

“A wonderful visit to pioneering mental health charity @dose_of_nature_, which supports people experiencing mental health challenges through a clinically led program that combines psychology, connection with nature, and one-to-one guidance,” she wrote. “Founder Dr Alison Greenwood and Clinical Lead Dr Georgina Gould share how spending time in nature can help us feel both calmer and more alert, exploring the science behind this, from the sound of birdsong to air in a woodland.”

Kate has rarely let her natural hair texture take center stage over the years, but her curls did make an appearance during a 2012 visit to Tuvanipupu Island.

The Princess of Wales has long championed nature through her royal work. Last month, she announced a new project from The Royal Foundation Centre for Early Childhood focused on the natural world.

She said in a video, “New scientific research increasingly shows just how important nature is for our physical and psychological wellbeing. Nature is such an extraordinary environment for us all, and in this tech-driven, digital age, spending quality time outdoors has never been more important. This project is about exploring how nature, creativity, and relationships can best support the healthy development of babies and young children. We believe that taking a whole-child approach is important, and nature becomes a wonderful place to explore how we can successfully nurture their social and emotional skills—the skills that underpin lifelong health and wellbeing. My hope is that through this practical, nature-based research, we can build supportive environments that help make nature more accessible, benefiting babies, young children, and their families across the U.K.”
